Where a several judgment is proper, the court may direct judgment against one or more defendants or direct a severance. (C. P. A. §§209; 211; 474; Mun. 

1 For summary judgment and judgment on the pleadings, see pp. 157-160. For judgment on appeal, see Chapter XI. 

2 See p. 11. 229

NEW YORK PLEADING and praCTICE 

Ct. Code §127.) If the summons has been served on only some of the defendants, judgment may be taken only against those served. (C. P. A. §475.) 

Where in an action on a promissory note the maker is not served and the payee defaults, it is improper to enter judgment against the payee without entering an order of severance. Kriser v. Rodgers, 195 App. Div. 

394, 186 N. Y. S. 316. 

§397. Joint Debtors. 

Where the complaint demands judgment for a sum of money against two or more defendants alleged to be jointly indebted on a contract, if the summons was not served on all, the plaintiff may proceed against those served; and if he recovers final judgment, it may be taken against all the defendants. As to the defendants not served, the enforcement of such judgment is limited to satisfaction out of joint property. (C. P. A. §§1197; 1199; Mun. Ct. Code §132.) 

A subsequent action may be maintained against the defendants not served or not joined. (C. P. A. §§1185-1188; 

1201; Mun. Ct. Code §132.) 

Section 1197 of the Civil Practice Act does not apply to a case tried against defendants as joint tort feasors.

Where one of such defendant copartners was not served with the summons, judgment could not be entered against him, nor could partnership property be levied upon under the judgment. The defendant not served obtained an order vacating the judgment as to him. An appeal was taken by the defendant served and a new trial was granted. A subsequent amendment of the complaint changing the cause of action to contract was insufficient to obtain jurisdiction of the defendant not served and judgment in the second trial could not be enforced against him. Kittredge v. Grannes, 244 N. Y. 

182, 155 N. E. 93.

Separate judgment cannot be entered on a joint obligation. Nathan v. Zierler, 223 App. Div. 355, 228 N. 

Y. S. 170.

JUDGMENT 

231 

§398. Judgment by Default.³ 

If the defendant fails to appear or answer in an action, judgment by default may be taken as soon as the time during which the defendant could have appeared has expired. 

If some of the defendants fail to appear, judgment by default may be taken against them on application and notice to those who have appeared. Where issue was joined and the defendant fails to appear for trial, the plaintiff may take judgment by default at that time. When the defendant is an infant, judgment by default cannot be taken until 20 days have elapsed since the appointment of his guardian ad litem. If the plaintiff fails to reply to a counterclaim, the defendant may apply, on notice, for judgment thereon. 

Where the plaintiff unreasonably neglects to proceed in an action, the court, in its discretion and upon application of the defendant, may dismiss the complaint. If the plaintiff fails to appear for trial and the defendant has served a notice of trial, he may take dismissal of the complaint. (C. P. A. §§433; 491, 492; 494; 181; Rule 193; City Ct.

Act §29.) §399. Extent of Judgment. 

Where there is no answer, the judgment may not be more favorable to the plaintiff than that demanded in the complaint. Where there is an answer, the court may permit the plaintiff to take any judgment consistent with the case and embraced within the issue. (C. P. A. §479.) In an action for breach of express contract to pay absolutely or on a contingency a sum of money fixed by the terms of the contract or capable of being ascertained therefrom by computation only; or an express or implied contract to pay money received or disbursed, or the value of property delivered, or of services rendered by, to, or for the use of the ³ For judgment by default in matrimonial actions, see Chapter XIII. 

4 See p. 28.

defendant or a third person; and the complaint or notice on or with the summons demands judgment for a sum of money only, judgment may be entered by the clerk without application to the court. (C. P. A. §§485, 486; Rule 46; Mun.

Ct. Code §6, subd. 3-a.) 

The clerk may not enter judgment by default unless a complaint was served and filed in the action. Arnold, Inc. v. Mackey, 129 Misc. 643, 222 N. Y. S. 225. $400. After Personal Service of the Summons. 

If the summons and complaint or notice were served personally within the state, and the defendant has defaulted in appearance or pleading, the plaintiff may take judgment by filing proof of service or appearance and affidavit of default. (C. P. A. §486; Rule 189.) Proof of the claim must also be filed. If the complaint was verified, this is sufficient; if not, proof is furnished by affidavit or other competent evidence. (C. P. A. §487; Mun. Ct. Code §6, subd. 3-a.) $401. In Equity Actions.

In equity actions and others not specified in C. P. A. §485, where the defendant has been served personally within the state and has defaulted in appearance or pleading, application for judgment must be made to the court or a judge. (C. P. A. §§489; 1174.) §402. In Actions for Injuries. 

In personal injury actions or injuries to property, the damages must be ascertained by a jury in court or a writ of inquiry (sheriff's jury). On such reference or inquiry the defendant in default may prove facts, not amounting to a total defense, tending to mitigate or otherwise reduce plaintiff's damages. (C. P. A. §339; Duffus v. Bangs, 61 

Hun 23, 17 N. Y. S. 597.) In other cases under Sec. 489, the assessment may be made by the court with or without a jury or by reference or writ of inquiry. Judgment may

JUDGMENT 

233 be entered by the clerk, or a report back to the court may be made in the discretion of the court except where spe cially provided by statute. (C. P. A. §490.) Application may be made to special or trial term except in the 1st and 2nd judicial districts where it must be made to special term. (Rule 191.) If the defendant has appeared generally but defaulted in pleading, he is entitled to 5 days' notice of application for judgment. The defendant may serve a written demand on the plaintiff before application, and he is then entitled to 5 days' notice of a writ of inquiry, reference or assessment by jury. Such demand is not an appearance. (Rule 190.) In the city court a writ of inquiry may be had on 2 days' notice. (City Ct. Act §29, subd. 2.) In the municipal court, application for judgment by default may be made to the court and, upon proof of the cause of action, the court may make assessment without a jury or writ of inquiry. (Mun. Ct. Code §80.) These proceedings are all called "inquests." 

§403. After Service Other Than Personal. 

If the summons was served without the state or other than personally, judgment may be taken by default in the following actions only: (1) For breach of contract, express or implied, other than a contract to marry. (2) Wrongful conversion of personal property. (3) An injury to person or property in consequence of negligence, fraud or other wrongful act. (C. P. A. §§483; 

493; City Ct. Act §29.)

Before judgment by default in one of these three cases can be taken against a defendant who is a nonresident or foreign corporation if service was made without the state or other than personally, a warrant of attachment granted in the action must have been levied upon property of the defendant within the state before the order for publica-

tion was made. Proof of the levy must be filed before judgment can be entered. (C. P. A. §493; Rule 192; Dimmerling v. Andrews, 236 N. Y. 43, 139 N. E. 774.) In an action for a sum of money only against a nonresident or foreign corporation, the warrant of attachment must be issued, and levy made before the order for service by publication can be granted. The judgment binds the defendant only so far as it can be satisfied from the property seized. (C. P. A. §§493; 

520; Rule 192; Pennoyer v. Neff, 95 U. S. 714; Dimmerling v. Andrews, 236 N. Y. 43, 139 N. E. 774.) But where the defendant appears, the judgment may be satisfied out of all property within the county whether it was previously attached or not. (C. P. A. §237; Reed v. Chilson, 142 N. Y. 

152, 36 N. E. 884.) 

§404. Judgment after Trial. 

Judgment may be entered on a "decision" of the court, the "verdict" of a jury, or the "report" of a referee. (C. P.

A. §7.) As already stated, in certain cases it is necessary for the court to render decision on a special verdict or a report. Upon the application of the party in whose favor a general verdict is rendered, the clerk must enter judgment. A motion for judgment on a special verdict, a verdict subject to the opinion of the court or the report of a referee, may be made by either party. (C. P. A. §495; Rules 

194; 199; 201.) When double or other increased damages are given by statute, the verdict, decision or report must specify the sum awarded as single damages and judgment is entered for the increased damages. (C. P. A. §435.) 

§405. Declaratory Judgment. 

The supreme court has power in any action or proceeding to declare rights and other legal relations on request, whether or not further relief is or could be claimed, and such declaration has the force of a final judgment. Juris-

JUDGMENT 

235 diction is discretionary and the court may submit questions of fact to a jury. (C. P. A. §473; Rules 212, 213.) "Parties cannot come into court and secure free advice to guide them in making contracts and entering upon business ventures. There must be an existing controversy, and the court must have before it the parties to the controversy, and the subject-matter, so that an adjudication, when made, will serve some purpose." Post v. Metropolitan Casualty Ins. Co., 227 App. 

Div. 156, 158, 237 N. Y. S. 64, 66.

Section 473 of the Civil Practice Act "was designed to supply the need for a form of action that would set controversies at rest before they led to the repudiation of obligations, the invasion of rights, and the commission of wrongs." Post v. Metropolitan Casualty Ins. 

Co., 227 App. Div. 156, 159, 237 N. Y. S. 64, 68.

Where a husband obtains a divorce in Mexico, which is void in New York, and marries again in Connecticut, the facts justify a declaratory judgment establishing that the wife of his former marriage is still his legal wife, but in the absence of proof of real injury the court will not grant an injunction restraining the husband and the woman he purported to marry in Connecticut from holding themselves out as man and wife, or from using the family name of the husband. Bauman v. Bauman, 250 N. Y. 382, 165 N. E. 819.

Where the complaint states that the plaintiff is an illegitimate daughter of the defendant, who is the only person living who knows the circumstances of the plaintiff's birth, a motion to dismiss because the complaint does not state a cause of action will be denied, and plaintiff will be entitled to a declaratory judgment if the allegations are proved. Morecroft v. Taylor, 225 

App. Div. 562, 234 N. Y. S. 2. 

§406. Judgment on Submitted Facts. 

The parties to a question in difference which might be the subject of an action, being of full age, may agree on a statement of facts upon which the controversy depends, and submit the same for determination to a court of record which would have jurisdiction if the action were brought. 

The case must be accompanied by the affidavit of one of the

parties to the effect that the controversy is real and that the submission is made in good faith for the purpose of determining the rights of the parties. The submission must be acknowledged or proved, and certified, like a deed. (C. P. 

A. §§546; 548; Mun. Ct. Code §124.) 

If the controversy is the subject of a special proceeding, the court has no jurisdiction under this section. Adelphi Homes Co., Inc. v. State Tax Com., 216 

App. Div. 773, 214 N. Y. S. 296; De Leyer v. Britt, 212 N. Y. 565, 106 N. E. 57. 

§407. Judgment by Confession." 

Without action, a judgment by confession may be entered for: (1) money due or to become due; or (2) to secure a person against contingent liability in behalf of the defendant; or (3) both. A verified statement must be made by the defendant stating the amount for which judgment may be entered, and authorizing entry thereof, and stating the facts out of which the debt or liability arose. Any time within three years of the verification, it may be filed in the county where the defendant was a resident and judgment entered by the clerk; but such judgment cannot be entered after the defendant's death. Such judgment may be enforced as a judgment after trial. (C. P. A. §§540, 541; 

543, 544.) One or more joint debtors may confess a joint debt. If all the joint debtors do not unite in the confession, judgment may be entered and enforced only against those who confessed it; but an action may be brought against the others. (C. P. A. §542.) Judgment by confession may be taken and entered in the city court, and the municipal court. (City Ct. Act §16; Mun. Ct. Code §6.) Confession of judgment made by a partner on behalf of the partnership binds the confessing partner only. It does not affect the other partners nor their interest in partnership prop- 

See tender and offer of judgment, p. 197.

JUDGMENT 

237 erty. (Scanlon v. Kuehn, 225 App. Div. 309, 232 N. Y. S. 592.) 

§408. Judgment after Death. 

If either party dies after an accepted offer of judgment or after verdict, report or decision, or an interlocutory judgment, but before final judgment, the judgment is entered in the names of the original parties. A verdict, report or decision rendered against a party after his death is void and judgment may not be entered thereon. (C. P. A. §478.) 

§409. The Judgment-Roll. 

The judgment-roll is filed by the clerk when the judgment is entered and usually consists of: the summons; the pleadings or copies thereof; admissions; the notice of trial; the final judgment, and the interlocutory judgment if any; and each paper on file, and a copy of each order which in any way involves the merits or necessarily affects the judgment. Except for the summons and admissions, copies may be filed instead of the originals. In the case of default, the proof of service of the summons, non-appearance, etc., as detailed under Judgment by Default must be filed. If judgment be taken after trial, the judgment-roll must contain the verdict, report or decision, each offer of judgment made, and exceptions or case then on file. (Rules 202; 189; C. P. 

A. §§440; 445.) Interest on a judgment for a sum of money from the date of rendering to the date of entering must be computed and added to the judgment by the clerk. Interest due in contract cases, except breach of contract to marry, is included in the award of principal by the trier of the facts. The judgment itself bears interest from the date of entry; except in cases where money is directed to be repaid, when interest dates from the time of original pay ment. (C. P. A. §§480, 481; Klausner v. Queens Fur Dress- 

• But see p. 30.

ing Co., 130 Misc. 579, 224 N. Y. S. 333.) Where a counterclaim has been admitted or proved, the plaintiff may take judgment for the excess. (C. P. A. §§477; 488.) The judg ment-roll must be filed before it can be enforced or collected. (C. P. A. §503.) In a court of record, the judgment is not stayed or affected by omissions or defects in entering judgment or making up the judgment-roll. (C. P. A. §109.) 

Ordinarily a court may amend its judgments and orders; but a judgment, as a general rule, cannot be amended so as to revive the liability of a released surety. (Tousey v. 

Barber, 132 Misc. 861, 231 N. Y. S. 133.) The direction of §480 to include interest in a verdict, in a contract action, is mandatory and the judgment may be corrected at any time prior to execution. (Tedesco v. Geneva, 134 Misc. 222, 235 N. Y. S. 739.) If the parties in a municipal court action have appeared by attorney, it is improper to enter judgment without notice. (Lane v. The Mirror, 134 Misc. 767, 236 

N. Y. S. 273.) 

§410. Docketing Judgment. 

Each county clerk must docket a judgment rendered in the court of which he is clerk when he files the judgmentroll. (C. P. A. §501.) A judgment for a sum of money is not a lien on real property or chattels real until it is docketed in the county where such property is situated. (C. P. 

A. §§509, 510.) A judgment directing the sale of real property must be entered in the county where the real property is situated before the purchaser can be required to pay purchase money or to accept a deed. (C. P. A. §500.) A transcript of the judgment in a municipal court action may be filed in the county clerk's office if an execution from the municipal court is not outstanding. Upon such docketing, the judgment has the same effect as a supreme court judgment. Where the judgment debtor in a municipal court action is subject to arrest and imprisonment, the judgment,

JUDGMENT 

239 the docket and transcripts must so state. (Mun. Ct. Code §§130; 128.) A judgment of a district or a circuit court of the United States may be docketed in the same manner. 

(C. P. A. §502-a.) §411. Proceedings after Judgment. 

A judgment may be paid and satisfied, enforced, vacated and set aside, or an appeal may be taken. 

§412. Satisfaction of Judgment. 

Upon payment of a judgment for a sum of money and presentation of a satisfaction piece and the fees allowed by law for acknowledgment of a deed, the judgment debtor is entitled to a satisfaction of the judgment. The judgment creditor must execute and acknowledge it before a proper officer. The attorney for the judgment creditor may execute the satisfaction piece if the judgment is paid within two years of the filing thereof. The docket of the judgment must be cancelled and discharged by the clerk when the satisfaction piece is filed. The amount due may be deposited with the clerk who then cancels and discharges the judgment on the docket. (C. P. A. §§532; 530, 531; Rule 204;

Mun. Ct. Code §142.) If execution was issued on the judgment, the sheriff's release must be endorsed on the satisfaction piece. (C. P. A. §§536; 530, subd. 5.) 

8413. Proceeds of Infant's or Incompetent's Case. 

When the proceeds of an infant's or incompetent's cause of action for personal injuries, after payment of the attorney's fees allowed by the court, do not exceed $150, the court may order the proceeds to be paid to the father, mother or some other competent person, for the use and benefit of such infant or incompetent. When such net proceeds are more than $150 but less than $5,000, the court, in its discretion, may make an order wholly or partly dispensing with a bond and directing that a designated person

and the guardian of the infant or the committee of the incompetent collect and deposit such proceeds in the name of the guardian or the committee in a designated bank or similar company. Such proceeds may be removed or withdrawn only on the order of the court. (C. P. A. §980-a.) 

§414. Contribution From Other Tort-Feasors. 

Where a money judgment has been rendered jointly against two or more defendants in an action for injury to a person or to property, a defendant who has paid all or more than his pro rata share may now maintain a separate action for contribution from the other tort-feasors; or, where the parties have appeared in the original action, a judgment may be entered by such defendant by motion on notice. (C. P. A. §211-a.) 

$415. Enforcing Judgment."

A judgment may be enforced by serving a certified copy upon the party against whom it is rendered; and, if he refuses to obey it: (1) by execution, or (2) by punishment for contempt of court; depending on the nature of the case. 

In order to carry the judgment into effect a motion, on notice and proof, may be made to take testimony by deposi tion. (C. P. A. §293.) 

It is not proper to enforce judgment by contempt proceedings if it can be enforced by execution. Walters v. Reinhoudt, 130 Misc. 745, 225 N. Y. S. 123. 

§416. Execution. 

Execution may be used to enforce a judgment: (1) Where it is for a sum of money or directs payment of a sum. (2) Where it is in favor of the plaintiff in an action for ejectment or dower. 

7 For enforcement in matrimonial actions, see Chapter XIII.

JUDGMENT 

241 (3) Where, in an action to recover a chattel, it awards a chattel to either party. (C. P. A. §504.) 

Execution may not issue against the people. (C. P. A. §659.) 

§417. Kinds of Execution. 

There are four kinds of execution: (1) Against property. (2) Against the person. (3) For the delivery of real property with or without damages for withholding same. (4) For the delivery of the possession of a chattel, with or without damages for the taking or detention thereof. 

(C. P. A. §638.) §418. Execution against Property. 

Execution issues to the sheriff in supreme court actions and levy and return must be made within 60 days. In the city court of the city of New York execution may issue to the sheriff or marshal, except that an order of arrest or execution to replevy a chattel must issue to the sheriff. 

Execution against property issues only to counties where the judgment has been docketed. An execution for the delivery of possession of real property must be issued to the sheriff of the county where the property is situated. 

An execution for the delivery of a chattel may be issued to any county where the judgment-roll is filed. Executions upon the same judgment may be issued at the same time to two or more counties. An execution out of the municipal court issues to a marshal whose jurisdiction extends throughout the counties of New York City. Return is made within 20 days unless renewed. If a transcript of a municipal court judgment has been filed with the county clerk, execution is issued to the sheriff as in a supreme court

action. (C. P. A. §§636; 640; 648; City Ct. Act §28; Mun. 

Ct. Code §§14; 131; 135; 138; 140; 151.)

Levy on the personal property of a judgment debtor in the municipal court may be made by a marshal anywhere in New York City. Hecht v. Sanger, 126 Misc. 

735, 215 N. Y. S. 409, reversed on other grounds. $419. Time of Execution.

Within five years of entering the judgment, execution may be had as a matter of course. After this period, it may be issued only: (1) where an execution issued within the five years was returned partly or wholly unsatisfied or unexecuted; or (2) upon order of the court obtained on notice. (C. P. A. §§650; 652, 653.) In the municipal court the execution may issue within six years. (Mun. Ct. Code §130.) 

§420. Property Subject to Execution. 

The judgment must first be satisfied out of the personal property of the judgment debtor, but if this is insufficient, the balance may be satisfied out of his real property. This rule also applies when a warrant of attachment has been levied. Where a warrant of attachment issued in the action was levied and the summons was served on a nonresident or foreign corporation otherwise than personally within the state, and such defendant has not appeared, the judgment can be satisfied only out of the property attached. (C. P. 

A. §§520; 643; 645.) If all the defendants in an action against joint debtors were not served with the summons, the judgment as to those not served may be satisfied only out of joint property. (C. P. A. §1199.) $421. Joint Property.

In an action against joint debtors, service of the summons on one authorizes judgment against all found liable. 

Such judgment may be enforced against the joint property although the other defendants were not served and did not.

JUDGMENT 

243 appear in the action. (Sternberger v. Bernheimer, 121 N. 

Y. 194, 24 N. E. 311.) The same rule applies where joint property of nonresidents has been attached and levied upon. (Yerkes v. McFadden, 141 N. Y. 136, 36 N. E. 7.)

In an action where an attachment had been issued, the execution directed the sheriff to collect the judgment out of the attached personal property, and if that was insufficient, out of the real estate attached. Held, that so far as the real estate was concerned, the execution was void, and a sale under it conveyed no title. 

That the provision of Sec. 645, subd. 2, prescribing the form of execution in such case is peremptory, and the attached real estate could not be sold until the remedy against the debtor's personal property, both attached and unattached had been exhausted. Place v. Riley, 98 

N. Y. 1. 

§422. Personal Property. 

Personal property upon which levy may be made includes the following: 

(1) Money issued by the United States. (C. P. A. 

§686.) (2) Bills or other evidence of debt issued by a money corporation to circulate as money. (C. P. A. $687.) (3) Negotiable bonds or other negotiable instruments for the payment of money issued by a government, state, county, public officer, or municipal or other corporation. (C. P. A. §687.) 

(4) Personal property pledged for the payment of money or the performance of any agreement, unless the judgment debtor is unconditionally entitled to pos session. (C. P. A. §688.) The exemption of property where the judgment debtor is entitled to possession is probably intended to apply to property held under a chattel mortgage or conditional sale contract. (5) All goods and chattels not exempt. (C. P. A. $679.) 

(6) Earnings and income. (C. P. A. §684.) 

* See p. 245.

§423. Garnishee Proceedings. 

Execution against wages are called garnishee proceedings. Where an execution has been returned partly or wholly unsatisfied, application may be made without notice for an order of execution against the earnings, debts, income and profits of the judgment debtor if they amount to $12 or more per week. This execution becomes a lien and continuing levy upon ten per cent of such earnings and income until the execution and the expenses thereof are fully satisfied. Only one execution may be satisfied at a time. It is the duty of the employer to pay over the said sums to the officer presenting the execution, and he is liable to the judgment creditor for failure. Commissions, drawing accounts or percentages of profits are wages. (C. P. A. $684.)

Under §684, execution may issue against income due in the future from a trustee to a cestui que trust. The lien attaches whenever the day of payment arrives. 

Hamilton v. Drogo, 241 N. Y. 401, 150 N. E. 496. 

§424. Exempted Personal Property. 

Exemptions from execution in the case of personal property include: (1) When owned by a householder, certain necessary furniture, books, farm animals, food, wearing apparel, bedding, tools and professional instruments, except where the action was brought for wages of a domestic or for the purchase price of the article. (The exemptions on some articles are valid only up to specified amounts.) (2) The pay, grant or equipment of a person in the military service of the state or nation. (3) Property within the state enroute to or on international exhibition under the auspices of the U. S. (C. P. 

A. §§665, 666, 667, 668, 669.)

JUDGMENT 

245 A chose in action is not subject to levy and sale on execution. Bayer v. Doscher, 139 App. Div. 324, 123 

N. Y. S. 1096.

Alimony paid to a wife for her support and that of her children cannot be reached by a judgment for necessaries unless it is shown that a definite portion belongs to her and that such portion is not required for her nourishment and support. Maurice Baskin & 

Co., Inc. v. Howe, 225 App. Div. 553, 233 N. Y. S. 648. 

§425. In an Action for Wages in the Municipal Court. 

When an action for wages was brought in the municipal court within three months after the cause of action accrued and the judgment did not exceed $100, no property of the defendant is exempt from levy and sale on execution; and if the execution is returned partly or wholly unsatisfied, the clerk, on application of the plaintiff, must issue an execution against the person of defendant, who must be confined in jail. After 15 days he must be discharged and cannot be arrested again, but the creditor may enforce the judgment against the defendant's property. This section applies to female as well as to male defendants. (Mun. Ct. 

Code §139.) §426. Mortgaged Chattels. 

A chattel mortgage, which is not accompanied by an immediate delivery and followed by actual and continued possession of the chattel, is void with respect to all creditors of the mortgagor unless the mortgage is duly filed. 

(Lien L. §230.) 

A mortgagee, who unreasonably delays the filing of the mortgage, cannot save it by filing it previously to the acquisition of liens by creditors at large. Baker v. 

Hull, 250 N. Y. 484, 166 N. E. 175, and cases cited. 

§427. Conditional Sales. 

The provisions of a conditional sale reserving title in the seller are valid as to all persons except purchasers or

creditors of the buyer, who without notice purchase the goods or acquire by attachment or levy a lien upon them before the filing of the contract. (Pers. Prop. L. §§64, 65.) 

Property which is the subject of a conditional sale cannot be reached by execution unless levy is made before the contract of conditional sale is filed. Where the contract was filed after execution issued but before levy, the contract superseded the execution. Baker v. 

Hull, 250 N. Y. 484, 166 N. E. 175. $428. Third Parties.

If a person not a party claims property levied upon, he may make an affidavit and deliver it to the sheriff or marshal, who may then try the validity of the claim. (C. P. A. §§696-698; 103.) $429. Priority.

The execution first delivered to the officer has the preference as a lien on the personal property of the defendant. 

(C. P. A. §680.) 

There is no priority of execution on subsequently acquired property. Matter of Hazard, 73 Hun 22, aff'd. 

141 N. Y. 586, 36 N. E. 739. 

§430. Sale of Personal Property. 

Sale of personal property by virtue of an execution must be made at public auction, on 6 days' notice, and the property must be on view at the sale, unless otherwise prescribed. (C. P. A. §§660; 706, 707.) 

§431. Lien and Levy on Real Property. 

A judgment for a sum of money or directing the payment of money, properly docketed, is a lien for ten years on the real property and chattels real in the county, which the judgment debtor has at the time or acquires within the ten years. At the expiration of the ten years levy may be

JUDGMENT 

247 made on filing notice with the county clerk. (C. P. A. §§510; 

512.) "Real property" includes leasehold property, where the lessee or his assignee is possessed at the time of sale of at least five years' unexpired term of lease, and also the buildings, if any, erected thereon. (C. P. A. §708.) A judgment for a sum of money or directing the payment of money entered against a party after his death, is not a lien on the real property or chattels real of the decedent, but it establishes a debt to be paid in the course of the administration of his estate. (C. P. A. §478.) 8432. Exempted Real Property. 

Exemptions to execution in the case of real property include: 

(1) The family burying grounds. (C. P. A. §670.) 

(2) The interest of a person holding a contract for purchase of real property. (C. P. A. §513.) 

(3) That portion of the real property covered by a purchase money mortgage. (C. P. A. §514.) (4) Land and buildings thereon not exceeding $1,000, owned and occupied as a residence by a householder (married man or woman) having a family and designated as an exempt homestead. (C. P. A. §§671678.)

The exemption of a homestead survives the owner's death. In order to designate property as an exempt homestead, a notice or conveyance so designating it must be filed in the "homestead exemption book" of the county. (C. P. A. §§670, 671, 672, 673, 674.) The lien of a judgment attaches to the surplus when the homestead exceeds $1,000. Such homestead cannot be sold by virtue of an execution, but a judgment creditor's action may be maintained to procure a judgment directing a sale of the property and enforcing the lien on the surplus. The interest of the judgment debtor, not exceeding $1,000, is exempt for one year as the property sold was exempt, unless he

designates other property as an exempt homestead. (C. P.

A. §§676, 677.) §433. Sale of Real Property. 

Sale of real property by virtue of an execution must be made by the sheriff at public auction in the county where the property is situated. Three notices must be posted in the town or county 42 days before the sale and a copy published in a newspaper once a week for the six weeks. 

Within 10 days of the sale, the sheriff must file a certificate thereof with the county clerk and give a duplicate to the purchaser. Title does not pass immediately. 

Within one year the judgment debtor may redeem by paying to the purchaser the sum paid on the sale and ten per cent thereof, and upon such redemption the sale becomes null and void. If the judgment debtor does not redeem within the year his creditors may redeem by paying the purchase price and seven per cent within three months after the year has expired. Other creditors may redeem from the first redeeming creditors. If the property is not redeemed, the title vests in the purchaser as of the date of sale. At the expiration of the fifteen months, the sheriff must execute the deed and deliver it to the person entitled thereto. (C. P. A. §§660; 712; 716, 717, 718; 724-732; 748, 749.) $434. Execution against the Person.

Execution against the person is sometimes called body execution and is a civil arrest. Before an execution against the person can be issued to enforce judgment, an execution against the property of the defendant must have been returned wholly or partly unsatisfied, unless the judgment debtor is already actually confined by virtue of an order of arrest in the same action or an execution against the person in another action. (C. P. A. §766.) An execution cannot be issued against the person of a woman unless an order

JUDGMENT 

249 of arrest has been executed in the action. (C. P. A. §765.) 

A judgment against an officer of an unincorporated association for a claim against the association does not authorize an execution against his person. (Gen'l. Ass'ns. L. §15.) 

In an action against joint debtors, body execution may not issue against any of the defendants not served with the summons. (C. P. A. §1199.) A person served by substi tuted service in a municipal court action is not subject to body execution. (Mun. Ct. Code §23.) Section 765 of the Civil Practice Act restricts the right to execution against the person of a woman to a case where an order of arrest has been granted and executed in the action. Ritz Carlton Apartments v. 

Fried, 134 Misc. 370, 235 N. Y. S. 624. 

8435. Right to Execution against the Person.

The right to execution against the person depends upon the nature of the action, unless an order of arrest has been granted and executed in the action, and not vacated. (C. P. 

A. §764.) The actions in which arrest to enforce judgment may be made are the same as those already specified under provisional remedies. When the judgment debtor in a municipal court action is subject to arrest, the execution against property may direct that if property sufficient to satisfy the judgment cannot be found, the marshal must immediately arrest the defendant. (Mun. Ct. Code §§135, subd. 2, 136.) 

If the plaintiff is entitled to execution against the person because of the nature of the cause of action, such an execution may be issued on a judgment recovered against him for costs. Allen v. Fromme, 195 N. Y. 404, 88 N. E. 645; Davids v. B'klyn Heights R. R. Co., 104 

App. Div. 23, 93 N. Y. S. 285, aff'd. 182 N. Y. 526, 74 

N. E. 1116. 

9 See p. 163.

8436. The Arrest. 

The execution must require the sheriff to arrest the judgment debtor and commit him to the jail of the county until he pays the judgment or is discharged according to law. It may be issued to any county in the state. (C. P. A. §§647, 648.) In the city court, the execution issues to the sheriff; in the municipal court, to the marshal. (City Ct. 

Act §28; Mun. Ct. Code §130.) §437. Simultaneous Executions. 

The judgment creditor may not have an execution against the property and also one against the person of the judgment debtor outstanding at the same time. However, after the judgment debtor has been in custody 30 days, the judgment creditor may require the sheriff to discharge the judgment debtor. After the discharge, the judgment creditor may proceed to enforce the judgment by other means. 

(C. P. A. §§767; 771.) 

§438. Length of Imprisonment.

Three months is the limit for which a judgment debtor may be imprisoned on a judgment up to $500, and six months in all other cases. (Civil Rights Law §72.) $439. Supplementary Proceedings. 

Supplementary proceedings may be brought to discover whether the judgment debtor has property which may be applied to satisfy the judgment. The remedies afforded are: (1) An order or warrant against the judgment debtor after return of execution. (2) The same after issuing execution but before return. (3) A third party order made after issuing and either before or after return of execution, against a person who has property of the judgment debtor or is indebted to him. 

The order under (3) may be used alone, or with (1) or 

(2). (C. P. A. §773.)

JUDGMENT 

251 

$440. The Right to Maintain. 

The right to maintain any of these proceedings is given only if: (1) The judgment was rendered for a sum not less than $25. (2) The judgment debtor must have appeared or the summons must have been served personally or by substituted service. (3) Execution must have issued out of a court of record. 

(C. P. A. §775.) 

(4) The judgment must have been a personal one and not one in rem, and must have been rendered in a state court. (Matter of Maltbie, 223 N. Y. 227, 119 N. E. 389.)

These proceedings may be instituted against corporations. (Matter of Boucker Co. v. Callahan Co., 218 N. Y. 

321, 113 N. E. 257.) 

$441. The Examination. 

At any time within ten years of the return of the execution, the creditor may, on order, examine the judgment debtor concerning his property. At any time after execution is issued and before its return, the creditor, on proof that the judgment debtor has property which he unjustly refuses to apply towards the satisfaction of the judgment, is entitled to such examination. A person or corporation having personal property of the judgment debtor worth more than $10.00, or indebted to him in a sum exceeding $10.00, may be examined on order. (C. P. A. §§779, 780; 

785.) The order and affidavits must be served in the same manner as a summons. (C. P. A. §798.) 8442. The Order. 

The order requires the person to attend before a judge or referee at a stated time and place. (C. P. A. §§798; 786.)

§443. Place of Attendance. 

If the judgment debtor or other person required to attend is a resident, he cannot be compelled to attend for examination except in a county where he resides or has a place of business. (C. P. A. §787.) The proceeding is usually instituted before a judge of the court where the judgment was recovered; except that on a judgment of the municipal court the proceeding must be instituted before a justice of the city court or county court judge in the county, within New York City, in which the judgment debtor re sides or has a place of business. (C. P. A. §778.) (NOTE: 

In view of the removal of the civil jurisdiction of the county courts to the city courts within New York City, quaere whether such county judge now has jurisdiction in this instance.) 

§444. Procedure. 

As a matter of actual practice, the person to be examined is sworn by a court officer and examined by the creditor's attorney, and only if the witness refuses to comply is he taken before the court. The questions and answers are written down and signed by the person examined. The examination is usually adjourned to another date if sufficient information has not been obtained. Either party may produce and examine witnesses. When property of the judgment debtor is discovered, an order requiring delivery may be obtained if the property is in the debtor's control. Otherwise a receiver may be appointed to sue any third person to recover such property of the judgment debtor for the benefit of the judgment creditor. (C. P. A. §§783, 784; 790, 791; 793.)

The relation between a bank and its depositor is that of debtor and creditor and the amount on deposit merely represents an indebtedness. Section 793 requiring delivery of money belonging to the judgment debtor

JUDGMENT 

253 in the possession of the third person does not apply.

The only relief is an order under section 792 permitting the bank to pay over the amount on deposit to the sheriff. Rosenberg v. Gamma Chapter, P. L. P. Frater nity, 133 Misc. 624, 233 N. Y. S. 128. 

§445. Exemptions. 

Property exempt from levy upon execution cannot be taken by these proceedings. A trust established for the judgment debtor by some other person, and his wages for the past 60 days, if they are necessary to the support of his family, are also exempt. (C. P. A. §777.) 

The exemption of the earnings of a judgment debtor in Section 777 refers to earnings for personal services and does not include income from investments. 1101

Park Ave. Corporation v. Cornell, 133 Misc. 397, 232 

N. Y. S. 663. 

§446. Disobedience. 

Disobedience to an order or oral direction of a judge or referee in the course of these proceedings may be punished by contempt. (C. P. A. §800.) If a judgment debtor fails to appear when ordered to do so, he is usually served with an order to show cause why he should not be punished for contempt. When he appears in answer to the order to show cause the court may fine him as much as the amount of the judgment, unless he can show mistake or good excuse. 

The drastic power to punish for failure to appear for examinations in supplementary proceedings should be strictly limited to those cases wherein there is a deliberate disregard of the court's order. But where, after the debtor failed to appear on an adjourned date, the attorney for the judgment creditor wrote both the attorney for the debtor and the debtor asking when it would be convenient for them to attend and his communications were ignored, there is sufficient evidence of willful and deliberate default, and an order to punish the debtor for contempt will be granted. Credit Assets 

Corporation v. Rockmore, 135 Misc. 230, 237 N. Y. S. 503.

§447. A Warrant of Arrest. 

A warrant of arrest may be obtained by the creditor instead of an order for examination if there is danger that the judgment debtor will leave the state or conceal himself and there is reason to believe that he has property which he unjustly refuses to apply on the judgment; or the warrant may be granted during the examination. (C. P. A. 

§§781, 782.) §448. An Injunction Order. 

An injunction order may also be and usually is made by the judge who grants the order for examination or the warrant of arrest restraining any person or party from transferring or disposing of property belonging to the judg ment debtor. (C. P. A. §797.) 

$449. A Receiver.

A receiver of the property of the judgment debtor may be appointed on notice at any time after the issue of a warrant or order. The receiver must be a resident of the state. 

In this case title to the property is vested in the receiver; as to personal property, at the time the proceedings were commenced, unless the judgment debtor resides in another county, when title vests upon filing a certified copy of the order of appointment with the clerk of the county where he resides; as to real property, when the order of appointment is filed with the clerk of the county in which it is situated. 

The appointment of a receiver does not affect the title of a purchaser in good faith without notice and for valuable consideration, or the payment of a debt in good faith and without notice. (C. P. A. §§805; 808, 809, 810.)

A receiver of the property of a domestic corporation may not be appointed in proceedings supplementary to execution. Section 306 of the General Corporation Law specifies the only cases in which a receiver of a domes-

JUDGMENT 

255 tic corporation may be appointed,10 and a proceeding supplementary to execution is not included. Matter of 

Boucker Co. v. Callahan Co., 218 N. Y. 321, 113 N. E. 257. 

$450. Judgment Creditors' Actions. 

A judgment creditor's action may be maintained when an execution against the property of the judgment debtor has been returned partly or wholly unsatisfied. This action may be maintained against the judgment debtor and any other person" to compel the discovery of any money, thing in action or other property belonging to or due the judgment debtor or held in trust for him; 12 to prevent the transfer thereof or the payment or delivery to him or any other person; 13 and to procure satisfaction of the plaintiff's demand. (C. P. A. §1189.) This action may not be maintained against a domestic corporation. (C. P. A. §1196.) The final judgment must provide for the satisfaction of the sum due the plaintiff out of any money, thing in action or other personal property belonging to or held in trust for him, whether or not it might be reached by execution. An interest of the judgment debtor in a contract to purchase real property may also be seized. But property specially exempt by law from execution; money, thing in action or other property held in trust where the trust was created by a person other than the judgment debtor; and his wages for 60 days before the commencement of the action if he proves they are necessary for the support of his family, cannot be seized. (C. P. A. §§1191, 1192; 1196.) The apparent contradiction here is resolved by remembering that the statute is specific in stating what may be seized on execution and also property which is exempt therefrom. Property which is not specially exempt nor specially liable to 

10 See also p. 176. 

11 See p. 250. 

12 See p. 253. 

18 See p. 254.

execution, apparently may be seized after a judgment creditor's action. A temporary injunction may be had or a receiver may be appointed in this action. (C. P. A. §§1193, 1194.) 

§451. Contempt Proceedings.14 

Contempt proceedings may be used to punish a judgment debtor, who refuses or wilfully neglects to obey the direction of the judgment in the following cases: (1) Where the judgment is final, and cannot be enforced by execution. (2) Where part of the final judgment cannot be enforced by execution. (3) Where the judgment is interlocutory and requires a party to do or refrain from doing an act. (4) Where the judgment requires the payment of money into court, or to an officer thereof, except where money is due upon a contract or as damages for nonperformance thereof.

Under (4), if judgment is final, it may be enforced simultaneously with, before or after the issuing of execution, as the court directs. (C. P. A. §§505; 985; Jud. L. §753, subd. 3.)

A personal demand must first be made. This usually consists of the service on the judgment debtor of a copy of the judgment or order. If he refuses to comply with the judgment, a warrant may issue to commit him to prison until he complies, or is discharged according to law. (Jud. 

L. §756.) The length of imprisonment is the same as in cases of body execution.15

When an order is made for the payment of a specific fund, it is enforceable as for a contempt. However, 14 See also p. 215. 

15 See p. 250.

JUDGMENT 

257 if the defendant had no money at the time judgment was entered, it was impossible for him to turn it over and he cannot be punished for contempt. Walters v. 

Reinhoudt, 130 Misc. 745, 225 N. Y. S. 123. 

§452. Stay. 

Enforcement of a judgment may be stayed by order if good cause is shown and security is given. The entry and enforcement of final judgment are not stayed by an exception, the preparation and settlement of a case, or a motion for a new trial, unless an order therefor is procured and served or such stay is granted in open court at the end of the trial. Ordinarily stay is granted only during the perfection of an appeal or application for leave to appeal. (C. P. 

A. §§167; 516; 591; 594-598; 886; 1309; 1435, 1436; 1446; 

Rule 203; City Ct. Act §50; Mun. Ct. Code §§6, subd. 3; 

125; 159.) 

§453. Reopening Judgment. 

The court, in its discretion, and upon such terms as justice requires, within one year of notice thereof, may relieve a party from a judgment, order or other proceeding, taken against him through his mistake, inadvertence, surprise or excusable neglect. (C. P. A. §108; Mun. Ct. Code §129, subd. 2.)

The opening of a default judgment is in the discretion of the court and defendant must show by affidavit facts establishing a meritorious defense and present the proposed answer. Crouse Grocery Co. v. Valentine, 131 

Misc. 571, 226 N. Y. S. 613.

A motion to open a judgment taken by default because of the failure of defendants' attorney to answer when the case appeared on the day calendar will be granted, upon payment of costs, if it is shown that the defendants have a good defense and that the attorney's default was unintentional and due to carelessness. Marcus v. Simotone & Combined Sound and Color Films, Inc., 135 Misc. 228, 237 N. Y. S. 509.

§454. When Summons Served by Substituted Service. 

When the summons was served by substituted service other than personally without the state and the defendant does not appear, he or his representative must be allowed, upon good cause shown, to defend after final judgment within one year after personal service of written notice, or if such notice has not been served, within seven years after filing of the judgment-roll. If the defense is successful, and judgment or any part thereof has been collected or otherwise enforced, such restitution as the court directs may be compelled; but the title of property sold to a purchaser in good faith pursuant to a direction contained in the judgment or by virtue of an execution is not affected. 

This section does not apply to a judgment for divorce or for partition. (C. P. A. §§217; 1198.) In the municipal court, the motion must be made within two years of the entry of judgment. As to judgments recorded after September 1, 1930, the time begins to run upon the service of a copy of the judgment and written notice of entry thereof. (Mun. 

Ct. Code §129, subd. 2.) 

§455. Vacating and Setting Aside Judgment. 

A motion on notice to set aside the judgment for irregularity may be heard within one year of the filing of the judgment-roll. (C. P. A. §§521; 527.) Within two years a motion to set aside a judgment for error in fact not arising upon the trial may be made on notice by the party against whom it was rendered; or if execution has not been issued and the judgment has not been wholly satisfied or enforced, by the party in whose favor it was rendered. (C. P. A. §§522; 526; 528.) If the person against whom the judgment is rendered is 16 (1) within the age of 21 years; (2) insane; or (3) imprisoned on a criminal charge or conviction for a term less than life, the time of such disability is not a part 

10 See p. 24.

JUDGMENT 

259 of the two years, except that the time cannot be extended more than five years nor more than one year after the disability ceases. (C. P. A. §528.) If a party be dead, this latter motion may be made by his executor or administrator. (C. P. A. §523.) A person not a party to the action, having an interest in reversion or remainder may make a motion to set aside a judgment awarding real property or possession thereof for error in fact not arising on the trial. 

(C. P. A. §524.) 

It has long been settled that a judgment of a court which has jurisdiction of the parties and the subject matter is not open to collateral attack except for fraud in its procurement. Shaul v. Fidelity & Deposit Co. of 

Md., 131 Misc. 401, 227 N. Y. S. 163.

Before a judgment can be vacated by a court of coordinate jurisdiction upon the ground that the contract upon which it was based was fraudulently obtained, or that there has not been an honest and fair performance thereof, the defendant must prove that he was prevented by some act or contrivance of the plaintiff, or by some accident unmixed with negligence of himself or agents, from prosecuting his defense in the action. 

Mayor, etc., of N. Y. C. v. Brady, 115 N. Y. 599, 22 

N. E. 237. 

Where a party defaults intentionally and abandons his cause, a judgment will not be vacated. Colonial Fuel 

Corporation v. Kahn, 214 App. Div. 83, 211 N. Y. S. 50. "A defendant, applying to a court for an order opening his default, must show, as a condition precedent to the granting of the relief, facts establishing a meritorious defense, and an affidavit of merits alone is not sufficient." Fitzgerald Mfg. Co. v. Alexander, 200 App. Div. 

164, 168, 192 N. Y. S. 700, 703. 

§456. Relief from a Void Judgment. 

If an attempt is made to personally serve the defendant with a summons, but the wrong person is served or for some other reason the defendant is not actually served, the court never acquires jurisdiction of him, and a judgment rendered against him in the action is absolutely void.

Such defendant has several remedies: (1) He may appear specially in the original action and move to vacate the judgment. The granting of such motion is in the discretion of the court and is not res adjudicata. (Matter of Killan, 172 N. Y. 547, 65 N. E. 561; Foote v. 

Lathrop, 41 N. Y. 358.) (2) He may attack the judgment collaterally. (Dutton v. Smith, 10 App. Div. 566, 42 N. Y. S. 80.) (3) He may treat the judgment as non-existent and proceed to enforce any rights he may have. (Ferguson v. Crawford, 70 N. Y. 253.) (4) He may wait until an attempt is made to enforce judgment or to claim a benefit thereunder, and then attack it collaterally. (Bonnett v. Lachman, 65 Hun 554, 20 N. Y. 

S. 514.)

CHAPTER XI APPEAL §457. Nature of Appeals. 

An appeal is an application to a higher court for a review of a final determination of a lower court. In general the same rules apply to appeals in actions and appeals in special proceedings. (C. P. A. §634.) §458. Parties. 

The person appealing is called the appellant, the adverse party, the respondent. The title of the action is not changed, except that the name of the appellate court is substituted for that of the court below. (C. P. A. §561.) 

8459. Who May Appeal. 

An appeal may be taken by: (1) Any party considering himself aggrieved by the determination of the lower court. (2) Any person interested and entitled to be substituted in place of such party. (3) The representative of a deceased person, when duly substituted.

No appeal can be taken from a judgment or order rendered upon the party's default, but he may move to open the default and appeal from a denial thereof. (C. P. A. §557.) 

§460. To the County Court. 

Appeals from the justice's courts are taken to the county court. (Jus. Ct. Act §427.) 

261

§461. To the Supreme Court. 

Appeals from inferior and local courts not of record may be taken to the supreme court, if an appeal to some other court is not expressly given by statute. (C. P. A. §623.) $462. To the Appellate Term.

In New York City, appeals from the city court or the municipal court may be taken to the appellate term of the department in which the trial court is situated. The record and briefs on appeal need not be printed; they may be type written. (Const., Art. VI, §3; C. P. A. §627; App. Term 

Rules.) 

8463. From the City Court.'

According to Section 627 of the Civil Practice Act, an appeal from a final judgment of the city court, or from a court or judge's order specified in the City Court Act, §57, may be taken to the appellate term or to the appellate division. (C. P. A. §627.) However, the Appellate Division of the First Department has held that an appeal from the city court must be heard in the appellate term in the first instance, and may not be taken directly to the appellate division. (Katz v. Waneta Realty Co., Inc., 191 App. Div. 

509, 181 N. Y. S. 770.) As a matter of practice appeals from the city court in both the first and second departments are taken to the appellate term in the first instance. 66 the trial court had no more power to direct the appeal to be heard in the first instance by the Appellate Division than it would have had to direct such appeal to be heard in the first instance by the Court of Appeals or by the Supreme Court of the United States. There is only one provision of the Code which could authorize a review of a trial had in the City Court in the Appellate Division without first having gone 2 1 For appeal direct to the court of appeals on a constitutional question, see p. 269. 

2 But see p. 269.

APPEAL 

263 through the Appellate Term, and said provision is a survival of the conditions existing before the Appellate 

Term was created, evidently inadvertently overlooked when the Code was revised to meet conditions created by the readjustment of the courts of this department. 

That is section 1000 of the Code of Civil Procedure." (Now Rule 220.) Katz v. Waneta Realty Co., Inc., 191 

App. Div. 509, 511, 181 N. Y. S. 770, 771. 

Sections 56 to 59 of the City Court Act provide that appeals from the city court may be taken to the "supreme court." Here again, the appellate term is the proper court. (Stern v. Barrett Chemical Co., 124 App. Div. 377, 108 N. 

Y. S. 811.)

A further appeal may be taken to the appellate division on leave from either the appellate term or a justice of the appellate division. If the appeal is from an order granting a new trial upon a case or exceptions, the appellant must file, with his application for leave to appeal, his consent that judgment absolute may be rendered against him if such order is affirmed. (City Ct. Act §61.) §464. From the Municipal Court. 

Except where granted on the applicant's default, an appeal may be taken to the appellate term from: (1) A judgment in an action. (2) A final order in a proceeding. (3) An order granting or denying a new trial. (4) An order granting or denying a motion to open a default and to vacate a judgment entered thereon. (5) An order granting or denying a motion to vacate a judgment or final order on the ground that it was made. without service of summons or process. (6) An order granting or denying a motion to discharge defendant from arrest, to vacate or modify a warrant of 

3 For opening default, see p. 257.

attachment or a requisition to replevy or a warrant of seizure. An appeal from any other order may be taken only on leave of the justice making the order, upon a motion returnable 8 days after the making of the order; or, after denial of such leave, on permission of a justice of the appel late court. (Mun. Ct. Code §154.) 

An order adjudging defendant in contempt is only a motion in a municipal court action and is not appealable under $154 of the Municipal Court Code. Weishar v. Weishar, 239 N. Y. S. 82.

Section 627 of the Civil Practice Act states that an appeal from the municipal court may be taken either to the appellate term or to the appellate division. However, Katz v. Waneta Realty Co., supra, construed the statute to mean that an appeal from the municipal court, as well as an appeal from the city court, must be taken to the appellate term in the first instance. 5 After appeal to the appellate term, a further appeal may usually be taken to the appellate division on leave from the appellate term or a justice of the appellate division. Where the amount involved is three hundred dollars or less, except where it is claimed a constitutional right is affected, an appeal is limited to the appellate term, whose judgment is final and conclusive, and no further appeal may be taken. 

(Const., Art. VI, §3; C. P. A. §627; Mun. Ct. Code §154, subd. 8.) $465. To the Appellate Division.

An appeal to the appellate division is heard in the department which embraces the trial court, unless it is transferred to another department on order. (Const., Art. VI, 

§2; C. P. A. §§617, 618.) 

4 See p. 262.

For appeal direct to the court of appeals on a constitutional question, see p. 269.

APPEAL 

265 

§466. From the Supreme and County Courts. 

A final judgment, an interlocutory judgment, a court order, or a judge's order in an action or special proceeding in the supreme court or county court may be appealed to the appellate division of the supreme court. (C. P. A. §§608, 609, 610, 611; 622; 631.) 

§467. From Other Courts of Record. 

An appeal may be taken to the appellate division from any other court of record possessing original jurisdiction, if it is not expressly provided by statute that appeals therefrom shall be taken to a court other than the appellate divi sion. (C. P. A. §622.) 

$468. Time to Appeal. 

Practically all appeals must be taken within 30 days after service upon the attorney for the appellant of a copy of the judgment and written notice of entry thereof. The time of the party entering judgment or serving notice thereof runs from such entry of service. (C. P. A. §§612; 625; 

632; City Ct. Act §§60; 62.) An appeal from an order affecting a substantial right made by an inferior or local court may be taken within 60 days of such entry or notice. (C. P. A. §626.) An appeal from a judgment or order of the municipal court must be taken within 20 days after the entry thereof. (Mun. Ct. Code §156.) §469. Notice of Appeal. 

A written notice of appeal must be served on the adversary's attorney and filed in the office of the clerk where the judgment or order was entered. (C. P. A. §562.) On appeal from a judgment or order of the municipal court, the written notice must be filed and, in addition, proof of service upon attorney for the respondent. If respondent did not appear by attorney he is notified by the clerk. (Mun. Ct. 

Code §157.)

§470. Security and Stay. 

Ordinarily security is unnecessary to perfect an appeal, but enforcement of the judgment is not stayed unless security is given or an order is obtained. In the discretion of the court, security may be dispensed with or limited where the appellant is an executor, trustee or other representative. 

No security is necessary to stay execution of a judgment where the appellant is the people of the State, or a representative thereof. Unless ordered, security is not required of a municipal corporation. (C. P. A. §§614, 615; 568; 570, 571; Mun. Ct. Code §159.) $471. The Record.

The appellant or, in submitted controversies, the plaintiff, must furnish his adversary and the court with a copy of the record on appeal. For the contents of the record and service and filing thereof, see C. P. A. §§575, 576; 616; Rules 

229-238; Rules of the various appellate divisions. $472. Briefs.

Each party serves on the attorney for his opponent and files with the clerk of the appellate court a printed brief of the points of law and authorities therefor upon which he relies. (Rule 235; Rules of the various appellate divisions.) 

§473. Hearing. 

When the cause appears on the calendar of the appellate division, the attorneys for the parties may argue or one or both of them may "submit." When an appeal is submitted the determination is made on the record and briefs. (Rules of the various appellate courts.) 

§474. Disposition. 

Any appellate court may reverse, affirm or modify the judgment or order appealed and enter judgment thereon,

APPEAL 

267 or may grant a new trial. A judgment, which is affirmed or modified, is enforced by the original court. An order of reversal of the appellate division must specify whether the reversal was made upon the facts, or upon the law, or both, and also whether the decision was unanimous as to an issue or party. An order of the appellate division made upon an appeal must be entered in the office of the clerk of that court. A certified copy of such order with the original papers upon the appeal must be transmitted to the clerk of the county where the court from which the appeal was taken is situated. (C. P. A. §§496, 497; 584, 584-a, 585; 620, 621; Rules 237; 239; Mun. Ct. Code §163.) Upon an appeal from the city court, the appellate court has full power to review any exercise of discretion by the court or justice below. Upon an appeal from an order of the city court granting a new trial, on a case or exceptions, if the appellate court determine that no error was committed in granting a new trial, it must render judgment absolute upon the right of the appellant. (City Ct. Act §§59; 64.) The appellate court may enforce its order entered in a special proceeding or it may direct the proceedings to be remitted to the court or judge below for enforcement. Ordinarily the order of the appellate court is enforced as is an order or judgment of the lower court. (C. P. A. §633; Mun. Ct. Code §161.) In the case of a reversal, restitution must be made; but the title of a purchaser in good faith and for value is not affected. The value or purchase price must be paid by the seller. (C. P. A. §587.) 

Facts found by the trial court upon request are for the consideration of the appellate courts, even though they were not incorporated in the formal decision. Elterman v. Hyman, 192 N. Y. 113, 84 N. E. 937; Bremer v. Manhattan Ry. Co., 191 N. Y. 333, 84 N. E. 59; Barcalo Mfg. Co. v. Maldonado & Co., 221 N. Y. 499, 116 

N. E. 345.

If the case contains a certificate that it contains all the evidence, no exceptions are necessary. Peo. ex rel.

De Laney v. Mount St. Joseph's Academy, 198 App. Div. 

280, 190 N. Y. S. 289, aff'd. 234 N. Y. 565, 138 N. E. 448.

The appellate division may review questions of law or fact in the absence of an exception if a substantial injustice has been done. Raible v. Hygienic Ice & Refrigerating Co., 134 App. Div. 705, 119 N. Y. S. 138;

Muldoon v. Dock Contracting Co., 199 App. Div. 733, 

192 N. Y. S. 19, aff'd. 235 N. Y. 553, 139 N. E. 731; Garlinger v. Linwood Const. Co., 206 App. Div. 107, 200 N. 

Y. S. 423.

Where the findings of the trial court fix property damages, but not damages for personal injuries the appellate division should grant a new trial rather than fix such damages. Sporborg v. State, 226 App. Div. 113, 234 N. Y. S. 476.

If the appellate division find there is not a sufficient decision stating the facts and conclusions of the court, all questions of law and fact are presented for review, and the court may direct such judgment as the facts warrant. Lyon v. Water Commissioners, 224 App. Div. 

568, 232 N. Y. S. 26.

A constitutional question cannot be raised for the first time in an appellate court. It must have been raised on the trial. Nathan v. Equitable Trust Co., 250 N. Y. 

250, 165 N. E. 282; Dodge v. Cornelius, 168 N. Y. 242, 61 N. E. 244. 

The appellate division has jurisdiction to review questions of law or fact even though no exceptions were made during the trial. However, the power of the court to reverse without an exception or where the question was not raised in the court below will be sparingly exercised and only in extreme cases. Raible v. Hygienic Ice & Refrigerating Co., 134 App. Div. 705, 119 N. Y. S. 138. 

§475. To the Court of Appeals. 

An appeal may be taken to the court of appeals as of right: (1) From a final judgment or final order of the appellate division wherein is directly involved the constitution of the state or federal constitution.

APPEAL 

269 (2) Or where one or more justices of the appellate division dissents from the decision of the court, "or, having sat in the case, refrains from participating in the decision of the court." The portion quoted has been declared unconstitutional. See next case. (3) Or where the judgment or order of the appellate division is one of reversal or modification. (4) From an order of the appellate division granting a new trial where the appellant stipulates that, upon affirmance, judgment absolute may be rendered against him. (5) From a final judgment or final order of a court of record of original jurisdiction where the only question involved on appeal is the constitutionality of a state or federal statute (on such appeal only the constitutional question may be considered and determined). Under this last rule, an appeal from the city court or from the municipal court may be taken directly to the court of appeals. (Const., Art. 

VI, §7; C. P. A. §588, subds. 1, 2 and 3; City Ct. Act §63.) 

That portion of §588, which authorizes an appeal as of right when one of the justices of the appellate division refrains from participating in the decision [see (2) above] is unconstitutional, since the legislature cannot enlarge the jurisdiction of the court of appeals. Charles 

W. Sommer & Bro., Inc. v. Albert Lorsch & Co., Inc., 172 N. E. 271.

An appeal taken from a city court judgment directly to the court of appeals is within the jurisdiction of the latter court when a constitutional question, and no other, is involved therein. Coler v. Corn Exchange Bank, 250 

N. Y. 136, 164 N. E. 882; Am. Historical Society v. 

Glenn, 248 N. Y. 445, 162 N. E. 481. 

§476. Leave Necessary. 

An appeal may be taken to the court of appeals from a determination of the appellate division, other than a final judgment or a final order, when the appellate division allows the same and certifies that one or more questions of law

have arisen which ought to be reviewed by the court of appeals (only the questions certified can be reviewed and certified answers are sent back to the appellate division). 

An appeal may also be taken to the court of appeals from a final judgment or final order of the appellate division, which is not appealable as of right under (1) through (3) above, where the appellate division certifies that in its opinion a question of law is involved which ought to be reviewed by the court of appeals; or where, in case of the refusal so to certify, an appeal is allowed by the court of appeals. Such appeal must be allowed when required in the interest of substantial justice. (C. P. A. §588.) 

Without leave from the appellate division, an appeal cannot be taken to the court of appeals from an order of the appellate division, vacating an award of arbitration on the ground of "evident partiality." Arcadu v. 

Levinson, 250 N. Y. 355, 165 N. E. 809. $477. Limitations.

The jurisdiction of the court of appeals under §588 is limited as follows: (1) No appeal shall be taken from a final judgment or final order of the appellate division in any civil case or proceeding originally commenced in any court other than the supreme court, a county court, a surrogate's court, a court of claims or board of audit, unless the appellate division allows the appeal by an order made at the term which rendered the determination, or at the next term after judgment is entered thereon, and shall certify that in its opinion a question of law is involved which ought to be reviewed by the court of appeals. (2) The jurisdiction is limited to the review of questions of law, except that it may review facts found by the appellate division, where the appellate division, on reversing or modifying a final judgment in an action or a final order

APPEAL 

271 in a special proceeding, makes new findings of fact and renders final judgment or grants a final order thereon. (3) No unanimous decision of the appellate division, made prior to July 15, 1926, that there is evidence supporting or tending to support a finding of fact or a verdict not directed by the court, can be reviewed by the court of appeals; but where in such a case prior to that date the justices of the appellate division from which an appeal is taken are divided upon the question as to whether there is evidence supporting or tending to support a finding of fact or a verdict not directed by the court, a question for re view is presented. (Const., Art. VI, §7; C. P. A. §589.) 

It is the duty of the Court of Appeals to review the facts when the findings and conclusions of the trial court were reversed and new findings were made and final judgment was directed by the appellate division. Boyd v. Boyd, 252 N. Y. 422, 169 N. E. 632. 

§478. Time to Appeal. 

An appeal to the court of appeals must be taken within 60 days after service upon the attorney for the appellant of a copy of the judgment or order and notice of the entry thereof. The party entering the judgment or order, or serving the notice, has 60 days from such entry or notice. (C. P. A. §592.) Where leave is necessary, the appellant must apply therefor to the appellate division at the same or next term succeeding the entry and service of notice. If leave is denied, the party has 30 days after service of a copy of the order denying such application with notice of entry, in which to apply to the court of appeals for leave to appeal. (C. P. A. §591.) 

§479. Notice of Appeal. 

The provisions for notice of appeal are the same as in the case of appeals to the appellate division. (C. P. A. §562.)

$480. Security.

Ordinarily security must be furnished to perfect an appeal to the court of appeals. Security is necessary to stay execution on a judgment or order for money only, for delivery of a document or property, or directing a conveyance, or for the sale or possession of real property. (C. P. A. 

§§593-598.) §481. The Record. 

For the papers which must be furnished as the record on appeal to the court of appeals, see C. P. A. §§577; 601, and the Rules of the Court of Appeals. 

§482. Briefs. 

The points of law and authorities therefor, upon which a party relies, must be printed and filed in the court of appeals and served on the attorney for the adverse party. 

(Rules of the Court of Appeals.) 

$483. Hearing and Disposition.

An appeal to the court of appeals may be argued or submitted as in the case of appeals to the other courts. (Court of Appeals Rules.) The court of appeals is limited to a review of the questions of law, unless the appellate division has made new findings of fact and rendered final judgment or order thereon, when the court of appeals may review the facts found by the appellate division. (C. P. A. $589, subd. 2; Matter of Flagler, 248 N. Y. 415, 162 N. E. 

471.) The court of appeals may modify or affirm the judgment or order, award a new trial or grant to either party a judgment to which he is entitled. (C. P. A. §604.) The judgment or order of the court of appeals must be remitted to the court below for enforcement. (C. P. A. §606.) In • See p. 267.

APPEAL 

273 the case of a reversal, the rule of restitution applies as in judgments of reversal in the appellate division. (C. P. A. 

The court of appeals cannot consider questions unless exceptions' were taken. Sugg v. Wandling, 250 N. 

Y. 517, 166 N. E. 307.

Where the appellate division reverses a judgment entered after trial without a jury and does not indicate which findings of fact are reversed or disapproved, it must be conclusively presumed, on appeal to the court of appeals, that the reversal was only upon the law and that the findings of fact were approved. Schnibbe v. 

Glenz, 252 N. Y. 7, 168 N. E. 444. 

7 For exceptions, see pp. 212, 224.

CHAPTER XII REAL PROPERTY ACTIONS ¹ 2 

$484. Authority for Appearance.

Where a nonresident defendant appears by attorney in a real property action, such attorney must file with the clerk written authority for his appearance, executed and acknowledged like a deed. A copy or notice of the filing must be served on the plaintiff's attorney with the notice of appearance or within 20 days thereafter. In such an action, the defendant may obtain an order, on notice, directing the plaintiff's attorney to produce evidence of his authority to bring the action. (Rule 55.) $485. Lis Pendens.

Whenever an action is brought for a judgment which will affect the title or possession of real property, the plaintiff should file, in the office of the county clerk, his verified complaint and a notice of the pendency of the action. Every prospective purchaser or incumbrancer is thereby notified of the action. If the lis pendens is filed before the summons is served, personal or substituted service of the summons must be made, or service by publication must be commenced, within 60 days after the filing. (C. P. A. §§120, 121.) 

§486. An Action to Foreclose a Mortgage. 

An action to foreclose a mortgage may be brought by the mortgagee or his successor in interest without first obtaining a judgment on the debt. However, if such action is maintained, foreclosure cannot be commenced before exe- 

1 For venue, see Chapter III. 

2 For service of the summons without the state, see p. 99. 274

REAL PROPERTY ACTIONS 

275 cution on such judgment has been returned partly or wholly unsatisfied. The complaint in a foreclosure action must state whether an action on the debt has been brought, and, if it has, whether any part of the debt has been collected. 

(C. P. A. §1077; Rule 255.) If the foreclosure action is pending, no other action on the debt may be brought without leave of the court. (C. P. A. §1078.)

The defendants must include: All persons having an interest in the real property subject and subordinate (subsequent) to the plaintiff's claim. The people of the State of New York, where they have an interest subsequent to the mortgage, or where the state has a possible claim for a franchise tax, may be made defendants the same as private persons. Any person whose claim is prior or superior to the plaintiff's claim may be made a defendant. (C. P. A. $1079; Tax L. §197.)

It is unnecessary to bring in the tenants as defendants in a foreclosure action, but if the tenants are not brought in, their interests are unaffected by the foreclosure. Douglas v. Kohart, 196 App. Div. 84, 187 N. Y. 

S. 102; Robinson v. Hartley, 134 Misc. 703, 236 N. Y. S. 361. 

At least 20 days before final judgment in the foreclosure action is rendered, the plaintiff must file a notice of pendency of the action (lis pendens) with the clerk of the county where the property is situated; and proof thereof must appear on motion for the judgment. (C. P. A. §1080; 

Rule 258.)

Payment of the claim due and costs by a defendant at any time before final judgment results in the dismissal of the complaint. The foreclosure is stayed until subsequent default by payment of the claim and expenses after judgment and before sale. (C. P. A. §§1081; 1084.)

If the defendant defaults or admits plaintiff's right, the court may determine the amount due, or order a refer-

ence for that purpose. On default of the defendant, the plaintiff may, on notice, apply to special term for judgment. (Rules 256, 257.)

Final judgment must direct the sale of the real property or such part of it as is sufficient to discharge the mortgage debt; except where the action is brought to foreclose or extinguish a right of redemption, in which case the final judgment fixes a time during which the property must be redeemed. This latter case is called a "strict-foreclosure." 

(C. P. A. §§1082; 1086.) The final judgment may include judgment for any deficiency remaining after sale, if the defendant appeared in the action or the summons was served personally. (C. P. A. §1083.) Any surplus remaining after sale must be distributed according to the rights thereto. (C. P. A. §§1082; 1088; Rule 261.) 

§487. Foreclosure by Advertisement.
