Where the mortgage gives the mortgagee or some other person power to sell the mortgaged property on default on the mortgage, such person may sell when default occurs upon giving proper notice. (Real. Prop. L. §§540-563.) 

$488. Action for Partition.

Where two or more persons hold and are in possession of real property as joint tenants or as tenants in common, in which any of them has an estate of inheritance, or for life, or for years, any one or more of them may maintain an action for the partition of the property; and for a sale thereof, if it appears that a partition will result in great prejudice to the owners. (C. P. A. §1012.) An infant may not bring an action for partition without the express permission of the surrogate of the county where the property is situated and also the approval of the court where partition is brought. Both must be satisfied that the partition will promote the infant's interests. A guardian ad

REAL PROPERTY ACTIONS 

277 litem must be appointed by the court. (C. P. A. §§1015; 202.)

Parties defendant must comprise practically every one having an interest in the property. (C. P. A. §§1017-1021.) 

The summons must be served personally or by publication. (C. P. A. §1033.) Substituted service is not effective in a partition action. (Toole v. Larkin, 206 App. Div. 809, 201 N. Y. S. 952.) The issues of fact are triable by a jury. 

Unless the court directs that the issues be framed, the issues are tried on the pleadings. (C. P. A. §1023.) The action is noticed for special term and the judgment is entered there on the verdict of a jury, if there was one. (C. P. A. §429.) 

An action for partition should be noticed for trial at Special Term, Part III. A motion may be made for an order directing that the questions arising on the issues of fact be distinctly and plainly stated. A certified copy of such order should be filed with the calendar clerk of Trial Term. The verdict must be certified by the clerk to the court at Special Term. Sinclair v. Purdy, 195 App. Div. 398, 186 N. Y. S. 273.

An interlocutory judgment determining the rights of the parties and directing partition by three disinterested freeholding commissioners is rendered. Where partition cannot be had without great prejudice to the owners, the interlocutory judgment will direct the sale of the property or a part thereof. (C. P. A. §1024.) Final judgment is entered on the report of the commissioners after partition or sale. (C. P. A. §§1033; 1058.) 

The title or interest of any party in the property may be put in issue. When a defendant, not a tenant in common or joint tenant with the plaintiff, puts in issue the title or interest of the plaintiff, all subsequent proceedings as to such defendant are the same as an ejectment. (C. P. 

A. § 1022.)

$489. An Action to Quiet Title. 

An action to quiet title to real property may be brought by a person who has been in possession of real property for one year, or of any undivided interest therein, claiming it in fee, or for life, or for a term of years not less than ten. In these actions the plaintiff seeks to extinguish the right of any one claiming adversely to him. (Real. Prop. 

Law §§500-512.) §490. Ejectment. 

Ejectment, now called an action to recover real property, may be brought by a person having an estate in the real property in fee, or for life, or for a term of years. (C. P. A. § 7; Rule 241.)

The occupant, if there is one, must be made defendant. 

If the property is not occupied, the action must be brought against some person exercising acts of ownership, claiming title to the property, or an interest therein at the time of the commencement of the action. Any other person claiming adversely to the plaintiff may be joined as defendant. (C. P. A. §996.) A lis pendens should be filed to protect the plaintiff against transference by the defendant during the action. (C. P. A. §§1008, 1009.) 

Where the plaintiff is entitled to the property or possession thereof, the recovery of damages is limited to the rents and profits, or the value of the use and occupation, for a term not exceeding six years. Permanent improvements made in good faith by the defendant may be set off against the claim for damages. (C. P. A. §1011.)

Where the right of re-entry on non-payment of rent obtains, and the defendant is six months or more in arrears, the grantor, lessor, or his representative, may maintain an action to recover the property without demand of the rent or re-entry. (C. P. A. §997.) In other cases where the right of re-entry is reserved, and in default of sufficient goods and chattels to distrain for the rent due, the plaintiff

REAL PROPERTY ACTIONS 

279 may re-enter, or may maintain an action to recover the property, on 15 days' notice to the defendant. (C. P. A. §998.) At any time before final judgment in these cases, the defendant may pay or tender the plaintiff or his attorney, or pay into court, the rent in arrears with interest and costs, and obtain dismissal of the complaint. (C. P. A. $999.) 

§491. Dispossess. 

The general provisions for summary dispossess proceedings are given in §§1410-1447 of the Civil Practice Act. $492. Emergency Rent Laws.

The "Emergency Rent Laws" passed by the State Legislature expired in 1929. An attempt to continue their provisions was made by the municipal assembly of the City of New York. (Local Laws of the City of New York, 1929,

No. 9.) This local law was declared unconstitutional by the Appellate Term of the First Department. (Gennis v. 

Milano, 135 Misc. 209, 237 N. Y. S. 432.) Thereafter the 

1930 legislature enacted Section 1436-a of the Civil Practice 

Act, effective September 1, 1930, which contains provisions similar to those of the old emergency rent laws. This section provides that, in the discretion of the court, a tenant may obtain a stay of a warrant of eviction for not more than six months by: 

A. Showing to the court that (1) The premises are used for dwelling purposes. (2) The application is made in good faith. (3) The applicant has not been able to secure suitable and similar premises in the same neighborhood, or that it would occasion extreme hardship to him or his family if the stay were not granted. 

B. Depositing in court. (1) All sums for rent overdue.

(2) A sum equivalent to the rent, which may be fixed by the court on the basis of the old rental or on the reasonable value, for the period of the stay or an installment thereof, as the court directs. 

These provisions do not apply if: 

(1) The tenant is objectionable. 

(2) The landlord proves that he intends to demolish the building for the purpose of erecting a new building on the premises. (C. P. A. 

§1436-a.) 

A waiver of these provisions in a lease or agreement is void. (C. P. A. §1436-a.) §493. Dower and Courtesy. 

An action for dower may be maintained by a widow for one-third of all lands whereof her husband, at any time during the marriage and prior to September 1, 1930, was seized of an estate of inheritance, provided the parties were married prior to that date. After August 31, 1930, no other action for dower may be maintained. (Real Prop. L. §190.) The dower right is extinguished by a judgment for divorce obtained against the wife; or by annulment of the marriage. (C. P. A. §1158; Grotsch v. Hassey, 133 Misc. 373,231 N.Y.S. 

469.) An action to establish and determine the value of an inchoate right of dower may be maintained by the wife where the husband has transferred the property fraudulently and with intent to defeat her dower right. (Simar v. Canaday, 53 N. Y. 298; Clifford v. Kampfe, 147 N. Y. 383, 42 N. E. 

1; Douglas v. Douglas, 11 Hun 406; Stokes v. Stokes, 119 

Misc. 168, 194 N. Y. S. 983.) But no inchoate right of dower will exist where the marriage is contracted after August 31, 1930, or where the lands are acquired after that date. (Real prop. L. §190.) There will be no right of courtesy in the real property of a wife dying after August 

31, 1930. (Real Prop. L. §189.) For the rights of a widow or widower to the property of the deceased husband or wife after that date, see Decedent Estate Law, Articles 2 and 3.

CHAPTER XIII MATRIMONIAL ACTIONS §494. Kinds of Actions. 

Matrimonial actions are entitled: 

(1) An action to annul a marriage. (2) An action for divorce. (3) An action for separation. 

§495. Summons and Complaint. 

The summons and complaint, or summons when served alone, in each case must have the appropriate title written across the face. (C. P. A. §1167.) When the summons is served personally, the person making the service must have definite knowledge that the person served is the defendant in the action. The source of such knowledge must be stated in his affidavit of service. (Rule 53.)

It is not proper to unite in the same complaint a cause of action for divorce and one for separation. Conrad v. Conrad, 124 App. Div. 780, 109 N. Y. S. 387. 

Purvis v.

An order for substituted service of the summons may not be had in a matrimonial action. 

Purvis, 167 App. Div. 717, 153 N. Y. S. 269. 

§496. Annulment of Void Marriage. 

An action to declare a marriage a nullity may be maintained where the marriage is void, i. e.: (1) It is incestuous; or (2) If it was contracted by a person whose husband or wife by a former marriage is still living unless: (a) Such former marriage was annulled or dissolved on any other ground than the adultery of the person remarrying. 281

(b) Such former marriage was dissolved on the ground of the adultery of the person remarrying and permission to remarry has been granted by the court. (c) Such former marriage was dissolved on the ground of absence. (d) Either party thereto has been imprisoned for life. A pardon does not restore the marital status. (Dom. 

Rel. L. §§5, 6; 7-a; 58; C. P. A. §1132.) $497. Annulment of Voidable Marriage.

An action to annul a marriage may be brought where the marriage is voidable, i. e.: (1) In the discretion of the court, when either party is under the age of legal consent (18 years).. (2) Either party is incapable of consenting to a marriage for want of understanding. (3) Either party is incapable of entering into the married state from physical cause. (4) Either party consents to such marriage by reason of force, duress or fraud. (5) Either party has been incurably insane for a perod of five years or more; provided, however, that if the marriage be annulled on the ground of insanity of the wife, the court must exact security for her suitable care and maintenance during life. s on

Judgment under subdivision (5) cannot be had until, in addition to other proof, an examination of the a eged insane party shall have been made by three physi ians, appointed by the court, who are recognized authorit mental diseases, one of whom is a superintendent of a hospital for the insane, and all of whom shall have that such party is incurably insane and so reported court. (Dom. Rel. L. §7; C. P. A. §1132.) 

state reed the

MATRIMONIAL ACTIONS 

283 $498. Infancy.

An action brought on the ground that one or both parties were under the age of legal consent may be maintained by the infant, either of his parents, or his guardian, or the court may allow any person as the next friend of the infant to maintain the action. But a party who was of the age of legal consent when the marriage was contracted, or a party who freely cohabited with the other party after attaining the age of legal consent, may not maintain the action. (C. P. A. §1133.) 

$499. Former Marriage.

Where the ground is that a former marriage still exists, either party may maintain the action during the lifetime of the other; or it may be maintained by the husband or wife of the former marriage. (C. P. A. §1134.) $500. Idiocy.

Where one party was an idiot, the action may be maintained at any time during the lifetime of either party by any relative of the idiot who has an interest to avoid the marriage. If a relative does not bring the action, it may be brought by a next friend of the idiot. (C. P. A. §§1136; 1138.) 

§501. Lunacy. 

Where a party was a lunatic, the action may be maintained at any time during the continuance of the lunacy, or, after the death of the lunatic in that condition, and during the life of the other party, by any relative of the lunatic who has an interest to avoid the marriage. If no relative brings the action, a next friend of the idiot may. Such an action may also be maintained by the lunatic at any time after restoration to a sound mind; but in that case the marriage should not be annulled if it appears that the

parties freely cohabited after restoration. Where one of the parties to a marriage was a lunatic at the time of the marriage the action may also be maintained by the other party at any time during the continuance of the lunacy, if the plaintiff did not know of the lunacy at the time of the marriage. (C. P. A. §§1137, 1138.) 

$502. Force, Duress or Fraud.

Where consent of one of the parties was obtained by force, duress or fraud, the action may be maintained at any time by the party whose consent was so obtained. Such an action may also be maintained, during the lifetime of the other party, by the parent or guardian of the person of the party whose consent was so obtained, or by his relative who has an interest to avoid the marriage. But the action cannot be brought, in the case of force or duress, if the parties voluntarily cohabited; or, in the case of fraud, the parties voluntarily cohabited with full knowledge of the facts constituting the fraud. (C. P. A. §1139.) 

Where defendant refuses to keep his promise that a religious ceremony would follow the civil marriage, the plaintiff may obtain an annulment on the ground of fraud. Rutstein v. Rutstein, 221 App. Div. 70, 222 N. 

Y. S. 688.

An adjudged incompetent is incapable of deception, and intent to defraud; therefore, the other party to the marriage cannot maintain an action to annul on the ground of the fraud of the incompetent. Chaddock v. 

Chaddock, 130 Misc. 900, 226 N. Y. S. 152. 

§503. Physical Incapacity. 

An action for annulment may be brought, where one of the parties was physically incapable of entering into the marriage state, by the other against the incapable party; or by the incapable party if he was unaware of the incapacity or did not know that it was incurable. The action can be

MATRIMONIAL ACTIONS 

285 maintained only when the incapacity continues and is in curable. (C. P. A. §1141.) $504. Necessary Jurisdictional Facts. 

An action to annul a marriage may be maintained only: (1) Where both parties are residents of the state when the action is commenced. (2) Where the parties were married within the state and the plaintiff is a resident when the action is commenced. (3) Where the parties were married without the state and the plaintiff is, and has been, a resident for one year when the action is commenced. (C. P. A. §1146-a.) §505. Alimony and Fees.

A wife suing for an annulment is not entitled to alimony and counsel fees; but a defendant wife is entitled to both. (Jones v. Brinsmade, 183 N. Y. 258, 76 N. E. 22.) 

§506. Trial. 

A jury trial of the issues of fact in an action to annul a marriage, except in the case of physical incapacity, may be had on application of either party, or without application, if the court so directs. (C. P. A. §1142.) 

Separation cannot be counterclaimed in an action for annulment. Sorenson v. Sorenson, 122 Misc. 196, 202 N. Y. S. 620. 

8507. On Default.

If the defendant defaults in appearance, pleading or on the trial, proof of the allegations must nevertheless be made. The declaration or confession of either party is not alone sufficient proof, but other satisfactory evidence of the fact must be produced. (C. P. A. §1143; Rules 275; 283.) 

1 But see Consolidation and Severance, p. 195.

If the action is brought under §§1133; 1137; 1139, the plaintiff must prove that there has been no cohabitation as prohibited by such sections, except that under §1137 in lieu thereof it may be proved that the lunacy still continues. 

§508. Interlocutory and Final Judgment. 

An interlocutory judgment must be filed 15 days after the rendering thereof. In the discretion of the court it may provide for payment of alimony or for the maintenance of the children until final judgment. If costs are awarded they are entered as on a final judgment, but cannot be enforced by execution or punishment until the interlocutory judgment becomes final. (C. P. A. §1175.) The judgment becomes final as of course three months after interlocutory judgment is entered, unless the court directs otherwise. (C. P. A. §1176.)

In an action to annul a marriage, the judgment is a res remaining within the jurisdiction to the extent that the court retains the power to set it aside for fraud, and in a suit for that purpose to permit the service of a summons upon the wrongdoer, if a nonresident, by publication. Everett v. Everett, 22 App. Div. 473, 47 N. Y. 

S. 994. 

§509. Children. 

Children of the marriage are usually legitimate although under §1135 of the C. P. A., the court has discretion as to the legitimacy in certain cases. The court, by the judgment or subsequent order, may award the custody of a child to either party as the interests of the child require, and may make provision for his education and maintenance out of the property of either or both parents if the marriage was declared a nullity, and out of the property of the guilty parent, if a voidable marriage was annulled. (C. P. A. §1140.)

MATRIMONIAL ACTIONS 

287 

8510. Action for Absolute Divorce. 

A divorce may be granted in New York State: (1) On the ground of the adultery of the defendant. (2) On the ground that the defendant has been absent for at least five years and the plaintiff has no knowledge, after diligent inquiry, that the defendant is living. (C. P. 

A. §1147; Dom. Rel. L. §7-a.) §511. Necessary Jurisdictional Facts. 

An action for divorce on the ground of the defendant's adultery may be maintained only: (1) Where both parties were residents of the state when the offense was committed. (2) Where the parties were married within this state. (3) Where the plaintiff was a resident when the offence was committed and a resident when the action is commenced. (4) Where the offence was committed in the state and the plaintiff is a resident when the action is commenced. (C. P. A. §1147.) If a married woman dwells within the state when she commences an action for divorce, she is a resident, although her husband resides elsewhere. 

P. A. §1166.) (C. 

These jurisdictional facts should be alleged in the complaint. If a plaintiff husband wishes to question the legitimacy of a child he must raise it in the complaint and the issue is tried as any other. (Rule 279.) 

8512. Other Necessary Facts. 

Even when the adultery is proved, the plaintiff is not entitled to a divorce in either of the following cases: (1) Where the offence was committed by the procurement or with the connivance of the plaintiff.

(2) Where the offence has been forgiven. The forgiveness may be proved either affirmatively, or by the voluntary cohabitation of the parties with knowledge of the facts. (3) Where the action was not commenced within five years of the discovery by the plaintiff of the offence. (4) Where the plaintiff has also been guilty of adultery under such circumstances that the defendant would have been entitled, if innocent, to a divorce. (C. P. A. §1153.) 

Lack of procurement, connivance and forgiveness and that five years have not elapsed since the discovery, should be alleged in the complaint. §513. Alimony and Fees. (C. 

Pending the action, the court, in its discretion, may order the husband to pay the wife a sum of money necessary to enable her to carry on or defend the action or to provide for the support of herself and their children, having regard to the circumstances of the respective parties. (C. P. A. §1169.) The court, in its discretion, may require the husband to give security for such payments. If he fails to give such security, the court may order a sequestration of his property and appoint a receiver thereof. The rents and profits of such party may be applied to the sums awarded, and, if they are insufficient, the court may order sale of sufficient real estate to satisfy the requirements. 

P. A. §1171.) The custody of the children may also be awarded on order before final judgment. Such award may be modified from time to time. (C. P. A. §1170.) Where the defendant cannot be served personally within the state, sequestration and appointment of a receiver, an injunction or some other method of taking the defendant's real and personal property into the possession and control of the court should be used. Otherwise and award cannot be enforced. (C. P. A. §1171-a; Myers v. Myers, 131 Misc. 318, 227 N. Y. S. 13; Mathews v. Mathews, 247 N. Y. 32, 159 

N. E. 713.)

MATRIMONIAL ACTIONS 

289 

Alimony may be enforced by: (1) Contempt against the husband; (2) Sequestration; or (3) Reducing the accrued alimony to a judgment and issuing execution. 

When the husband has died during the pendency of an action for separation only the third remedy may be used to procure payment of accrued alimony out of the decedent's estate. White v. White, 224 App. Div. 355, 231 N. Y. S. 146. 

A defendant husband may be punished for contempt on failure to furnish a surety bond guaranteeing payment of alimony. Rosen v. Rosen, 226 App. Div. 327, 235 N. Y. S. 169; (overruling Moore v. Moore, 142 App. 

Div. 459, 126 N. Y. S. 936, and Pelz v. Pelz, 182 App. 

Div. 923, 169 N. Y. S. 430).

A receiver cannot be appointed upon a judgment for separation against a nonresident served by publication unless the court has laid hands upon his property within the state by attachment, by injunction, by sequestration or by some other means. Mathews v. Mathews, 247 

N. Y. 32, 159 N. E. 713.

A foreign judgment for divorce may be enforced in this state only as to installments of alimony due at the commencement of the action, and not as to future installments. Sections 1171 and 1172 apply only to judgments obtained in the United States. Boissevain v. 

Boissevain, 252 N. Y. 178, 169 N. E. 130. 

8514. The Answer. 2 The defendant may set up a counterclaim for divorce or for separation. The answer need not be verified; except that a counterclaim charging the plaintiff with adultery must be verified. (C. P. A. §§1148; 1168.) Either party may serve a copy of his pleading on the co-respondent named therein, and such person has 20 days in which to appear and defend. If the co-respondent is not served he may appear and demand a copy of the summons and complaint, which must be served within 10 days, and he may defend in so far as the issues affect him. 

2 See Consolidation and Severance, p. 195. 

(C. P. A. §1151.)

8515. Trial. 

A jury trial of the issue of adultery may be had upon the application of either party or may be directed on the court's own motion. For this purpose the questions to be tried must be prepared and settled. (C. P. A. §1149.) 

8516. On Default.

If the defendant defaults, the plaintiff must nevertheless satisfactorily prove service of the summons, the material allegations of the complaint and also that there is no judgment or decree in any court of competent jurisdiction against him in favor of the defendant for a divorce on the ground of adultery. (C. P. A. §1150; Rules 53; 277; 283.)

However, in a defended action, the defendant must plead and prove the material allegations which would preclude the plaintiff from obtaining judgment. Berndt v. Berndt, 130 Misc. 854, 225 N. Y. S. 404. $517. Interlocutory Judgment.

An interlocutory judgment must be entered within 15 days of the granting thereof by the court. In the discretion of the court, it may provide for the payment of alimony or for the support and maintenance of the children until final judgment. If costs are awarded, the amount is entered as upon a final judgment, except it cannot be enforced by execution or punishment until entry of final judgment. 

(C. P. A. §1175.) 

A marriage of one of the parties, contracted before an interlocutory decree of divorce becomes final, is void. 

Bamberger v. Bamberger, 128 Misc. 1, 217 N. Y. S. 675. 

8518. Final Judgment.

The judgment becomes final as of course three months after interlocutory judgment is entered, unless the court directs otherwise. (C. P. A. §1176.) It may require the defendant to provide for the suitable maintenance of the

MATRIMONIAL ACTIONS 

291 wife and children and for the education of the children. 

The provision may be changed on application from time to time. (C. P. A. §1155.) It provides that the plaintiff may marry again, but the defendant may not marry any one except the plaintiff during the lifetime of the plaintiff, unless the court grants permission on a satisfactory showing that three years have elapsed since final judgment, and that his conduct has been uniformly good. (Dom. Rel. L. §8.) 

Where the defendant had been forbidden to remarry and the ceremony was performed forty miles out at sea by a qualified commodore, the judgment of the state court had no extraterritorial force and the marriage was legal. Since the laws of the state where the owner of the ship is domiciled govern, and since the title to this ship was in the U. S. Shipping Board, the laws of the District of Columbia governed. Even if the ceremony was invalid, such marriage and the subsequent cohabitation in jurisdictions where common-law marriages are recognized (recognized by U. S. Congress and there is no showing as to District of Columbia) gave the parties the status of husband and wife. Fisher v. 

Fisher, 250 N. Y. 313, 165 N. E. 460.

The rule is inflexible that no allowance may be made, after the case is closed, for counsel fee for services already rendered or expenses incurred. Fisher v. Fisher, 223 App. Div. 19, 227 N. Y. S. 345. However, where an allowance was made in a separation action on condition that it would not preclude subsequent application for services rendered on the reopening of the case by the defendant, a motion for an additional allowance, at the time of confirming the referee's second report on the second hearing, will be granted. Guttas v. Guttas, 131 

Misc. 278, 227 N. Y. S. 456.

It is unnecessary to notify the plaintiff in the divorce action, when application for permission to remarry is made. Matter of Salmon, 34 Misc. 251, 69 N. Y. S. 215. 

§519. Enforcement. 

If the husband defaults in payment of an award in an action in this or another state, and enforcement cannot be

made by sequestration or similar method, the defendant may be punished as for a contempt. (C. P. A. §1172.)

A plaintiff suing on a decree of a foreign court granting divorce on the ground of cruelty, is limited under $1172 of C. P. A. to recovery of a money judgment for alimony due and unpaid. Miller v. Miller, 246 N. Y. 

636, 159 N. E. 681.

The final judgment will be modified if the husband's earning capacity has materially decreased so that he can no longer comply with the mandate of the court. Burdick v. Burdick, 183 App. Div. 488, 171 N. Y. S. 247. $520. Subsequent Marriage.

The subsequent remarriage of the plaintiff entitles the defendant husband to an order annulling the provisions of the final order directing payments for the support of the plaintiff. (C. P. A. §1159.) 

Payment of alimony ceases on the remarriage of the wife, but if her second marriage is annulled, payments must be resumed as from the date of the annulment. 

Sleicher v. Sleicher, 251 N. Y. 366, 167 N. E. 501. 

§521. Divorce by Absence. 

A divorce may be procured under the so-called Enoch 

Arden Law. Where a husband or wife has been absent, the other party to the marriage may present to the supreme court a verified petition showing: (1) That the party has absented himself for five successive years last past. (2) And during that time the petitioner has had no information showing the absentee to be alive. (3) That the petitioner believes the absentee to be dead. (4) That a diligent search has been made to discover evidence showing that such husband or wife is living, and no such evidence has been found.

MATRIMONIAL ACTIONS 

293 Notice of such petition is published like a summons, where service is made by publication, directed to the absentee and stating the time and place of hearing. The hearing cannot be had until 20 days after the last publication. If the court is satisfied, after hearing, that the allegations are true, it may make an order dissolving the marriage. (Dom. Rel. L. §7-a.) The jurisdictional requirements stated in C. P. A. 

§§1146-a, 1147; 1162, apply to proceedings for dissolution under §7-a of the Dom. Rel. L. In re Kohler, 123 

Misc. 305, 205 N. Y. S. 177. 

8522. Action for a Separation. 

An action may be maintined by a husband or wife against the other party to procure a judgment separating the parties from bed and board forever, or for a limited time for any of the following causes: (1) The cruel and inhuman treatment of the plaintiff by the defendant. (2) Such conduct on the part of the defendant towards the plaintiff as may render it unsafe and improper for the former to cohabit with the latter. (3) The abandonment of the plaintiff by the defendant. (4) Where the wife is the plaintiff, the neglect or refusal of the defendant to provide for her. (C. P. A. §1161.) 

8523. Necessary Jurisdictional Facts. 

An action for separation may be maintained only: (1) Where both parties are residents of the state when the action is commenced. (2) Where the parties were married in the state and the plaintiff is a resident when the action is commenced. (3) Where the parties having been married without the state, have been residents for at least one year; and the plaintiff is such a resident when the action is commenced.

(C. P. A. §1162.) A married woman who dwells within the state when she commences the action, is a resident, although her husband resides elsewhere. (C. P. A. §1166.) 

The complaint must specify particularly the acts of the misconduct of the defendant, and the time and place thereof. (Rule 280.) 

§524. Alimony and Expenses. 

The provisions for the custody and maintenance of children and the support of plaintiff wife in an action for separation are the same as in an action for divorce. (C. P. A. 

§§1169-1172.) 

$525. The Answer.

The defendant may set up the misconduct of the plaintiff as a defense, and, if the defense is established, the defendant is entitled to judgment. (C. P. A. §1163.) The defendant may counterclaim an action for divorce or an action for separation. (C. P. A. §1168.) Facts authorizing an action for annulment are no basis for a counterclaim or defense, but the separation action may be stayed until determination of the annulment action, or the actions may be consolidated. (Murphy v. Murphy, 194 App. Div. 395, 185 

N. Y. S. 536; Tanorozzi v. Tanorozzi, 118 Misc. 30, 193 N. 

Y. S. 352.) A wife's antenuptial agreement that her husband need not support her is invalid, and is no defense to the wife's action for separation. (Gregg v. Gregg, 133 Misc. 

109, 231 N. Y. S. 221.) 3 Refusal of a wife to submit to ordinary marital relations with her husband amounts to legal misconduct which is a good defense in an action for separation and support. The plaintiff is not relieved from her duty to fulfill the marriage contract, before she claims benefits thereunder, by the fact that an antenuptial agreement 

³ See Consolidation and Severance, p. 195.

MATRIMONIAL ACTIONS 

295 between them, that they would go through a religious ceremony following the civil one, was broken by the husband. This state regards marriage as a civil contract and the laws cannot be modified by a private agreement. 

Mirizio v. Mirizio, 242 N. Y. 74, 150 N. E. 605.

However, after this determination by the court, plaintiff notified the husband that she would submit to the law and offered to live with him as a wife in every sense and he refused, her previous separation from him during the former trial did not constitute abandonment such as would preclude her in a new action for separation. Mirizio v. Mirizio, 248 N. Y. 175, 161 N. E. 461.

Defendant's answer in a separation action cannot be stricken out because of his refusal to pay alimony and counsel fees as directed by the court. Such striking out would be in violation of due process of law. Sibley v. 

Sibley, 76 App. Div. 132, 78 N. Y. S. 743; Hovey v. Elliot, 167 U. S. 409; but the wife is entitled to a stay of his affirmative proceeding. Maran v. Maran, 137 App. Div. 

348, 122 N. Y. S. 9. $526. On Default.

On the default of the defendant in an action for separation, proof must be given of the allegations in the complaint. (Rule 283.) 

8527. Final Judgment.

The provisions of the final judgment are in the discretion of the court. It may compel the husband defendant to provide suitably for the education and maintenance of the children and the support of the plaintiff, according to the circumstances of the parties. The court may make such provision even though judgment for separation is not ren dered. (C. P. A. §1164.) §528. Revocation of Judgment. 

Upon the joint application of the parties, accompanied with satisfactory evidence of their reconciliation, a judgment for separation may be revoked subject to such regu-

lations and restrictions as the court thinks fit to impose.

(C. P. A. §1165.) 

A temporary reconciliation does not revoke a separation judgment so as to prevent recovery of alimony accruing after reconciliation ended. Revocation can be effected only in accordance with §1165 of the Civil Prac tice Act. Schatzberg v. Schatzberg, 229 App. Div. 214, 

241 N. Y. S. 684. $529. References.

An undefended matrimonial action may be referred to an official referee for hearing. Ordinarily the referee takes proof of the allegations of the complaint and reports to the court. The court then gives judgment. (Jud. L. §115; C. P. 

A. §§469; 1174; Rule 283.)

8530. Definition. 

CHAPTER XIV SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

A special proceeding is any prosecution, not an action, brought by a party, in a court, for the enforcement or protection of a right, the redress or prevention of a wrong, or the punishment of a public offense. (C. P. A. §§5, 6.) 

The most important special proceedings which are available when the ordinary civil action is inapplicable include: habeas corpus, certiorari, mandamus, and prohibition. An arbitration proceeding is not a "special proceeding" under the Civil Practice Act but is such proceeding as provided for in the Arbitration Law. 

§531. State Writs. 

Habeas corpus, certiorari, mandamus and prohibition were formerly brought on by state writs. The writ of habeas corpus could not be abolished by statute because it appears in the Constitution. An attempt was made to abolish the writ of certiorari, but it still appears in the statute for the purpose of inquiring into the detention of a person. 

Otherwise the writ of certiorari, as well as the writs of mandamus and prohibition, has been abolished and the order in each case has been substituted. These orders are issued in the name of the people of the state, as were the writs. 

8532. Writs of Habeas Corpus and Certiorari. 

A writ of certiorari demands an inquiry into the cause of the detention of a prisoner; the writ of habeas corpus not only makes this inquiry, but demands that the body of the prisoner be brought before the examining judge. The 297

former is used when the offense for which the party is detained is not bailable. (C. P. A. §1261.) "Under the writ of habeas corpus, the body of the person must be produced. If the offense is bailable, the court may then accept bail. If the offense is not bailable, there is no necessity of the presence of the detained prisoner upon the argument, and a writ of certiorari may be issued, which calls for precisely the same return from the custodian, but does not bring the body of the detained person." Peo. ex rel. Taylor v. Seaman, 8 Misc. 152, 153, 29 N. Y. S. 329, 330. See also, Peo. ex rel. Semenoff v. Nagle, 118 Misc. 476, 194 N. Y. 

S. 602. 

8533. Right to Writ. 

A writ of habeas corpus or certiorari may be obtained to inquire into the cause of imprisonment or restraint of a person who is imprisoned or restrained in his liberty within the state for any cause or pretense, and to deliver him therefrom. (C. P. A. §1230.) However, such writ may not be obtained by a person committed or detained by virtue of: (1) A mandate of the federal court. (2) A final judgment or decree of a competent tribunal of civil or criminal jurisdiction. (3) A final order of such tribunal in a special proceeding instituted for any cause except to punish him for contempt. (4) Or by virtue of an execution or other process issued upon such judgment, decree or final order. (C. P. A. §1231.) 

The privilege of the writ of habeas corpus can never be suspended, unless the public safety, in cases of rebellion or invasion, requires such suspension. (N. Y. Const. Art. I, §4.) 

8534. Use of Writ of Habeas Corpus.

The following cases illustrate the use of the writ of habeas corpus: where a prisoner is detained after his lawful term has expired or is imprisoned under a void judg-

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

299 ment; where bail has been improperly denied; where a sane person was committed to an asylum as a lunatic; where a lunatic has become sane since such commitment; or where a child is detained from a person having the right to its custody. 

When the relator was sentenced to life imprisonment as a fourth offender and the commitment showed a suspended sentence on one of the four alleged convictions, the sentence to life imprisonment was illegal and void, and the relator was discharged on a habeas corpus proceeding. Peo. ex rel. Rabideau v. Kaiser, 134 Misc. 

468, 235 N. Y. S. 629; Peo. ex rel. Friedman v. Kaiser, 134 Misc. 786, 236 N. Y. S. 432. But where sentence is pronounced and only execution suspended, the rule does not apply. Peo. ex rel. Bierbaum v. Jennings, 135 

Misc. 809, 240 N. Y. S. 91.

A writ of habeas corpus may be had to determine whether a person is held under a commitment for examination of an apparently insane person or held illegally under color of such commitment. Peo. ex rel. 

Ledwith v. Board of Trustees, 238 N. Y. 403, 144 N. E. 657. 

Sec. 81 of the Domestic Relations Law makes the father and mother joint guardians of their children. 

Where a father, during the absence and against the wishes of the mother, placed their children in a distant school, the mother was entitled, on a writ of habeas corpus, to the return of the children to the joint custody. Peo. ex rel. De Laney v. Mt. St. Joseph's Academy, 198 App. Div. 75, 189 N. Y. S. 775, aff'd. 234 N. Y. 

565, 138 N. E. 448. 

A writ of habeas corpus will not be granted to modify a final decree of divorce in respect to the award of the custody of the children. The proper remedy is an application to modify the decree, under §1170 of the 

Civil Practice Act. Peo. ex rel. Strauss v. Steindler, 

223 App. Div. 230, 227 N. Y. S. 726. 

8535. The Application. 

The application must be made by written petition, signed and verified by the person detained or someone in his behalf.

Where the people of the state are a party or are interested, the application may be made by the attorney-general or the district attorney. Where a judge authorized to grant these writs has evidence in a judicial proceeding before him that any person is illegally imprisoned or restrained, he must grant one of these writs without application. (C. P. A. §§1232, 1233, 1234; 1241.) $536. Who May Grant Writs. 

The following courts and officers may grant writs: (1) The supreme court at special term or the appellate division, if the prisoner is detained within the judicial district. (2) A justice of the supreme court in any part of the state. (3) An officer authorized to perform the duties of a justice of the supreme court in chambers, being or residing in the county where the prisoner is detained, or if there is no officer authorized within the county, to an officer in an adjoining county. (C. P. A. §1232.) 

8537. Time.

The writ must be granted without delay whenever presented including Sundays, unless it appears from the papers that the prisoner is prohibited by law from prosecuting the writ. Each judge who violates this section forfeits $1,000 to the prisoner. (C. P. A. §1235.)

A notice of appeal from a writ of habeas corpus directing the discharge of the prisoner is ineffective as a stay, and the prisoner must be discharged forthwith without bail or recognizance until the appeal is determined. Peo. ex rel. Sabatino v. Jennings, 246 N. Y. 258, 158 N. E. 613. 

8538. Parties.

The writ is issued on behalf of the people of the state, and if awarded on petition of a private person, must show

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

301 that it was issued upon the relation of that person. The officer or person against whom the writ is issued is styled the defendant. (C. P. A. §1236.) 

8539. Service. 

Either writ may be returnable forthwith or on a future day certain, but a writ of habeas corpus cannot be returnable on Sunday. (C. P. A. §1239.) Service is made by delivering the writ to the person to whom it is directed, and if he cannot be found, by leaving it at the place of imprisonment with any other officer or person of proper age, and tendering the fees for bringing up the prisoner. If the defendant keeps himself concealed or refuses admittance to the server, service may be made by fixing the writ to the outside of the place of confinement or the defendant's dwelling. A writ of habeas corpus may be served on Sunday; and by any person twenty-one years or upwards. (C. P. A. §§1242, 1243.) 

8540. The Return.

The person upon whom either writ has been duly served must make a signed return stating: (1) whether or not he detains the prisoner; (2) why the prisoner is restrained; (3) why the prisoner was transferred from him, if such is the case. The return must be verified unless made by a sworn public officer. (C. P. A. §1245.) If the writ was one of habeas corpus, he must also bring up the body of the prisoner in his custody unless he states in his return that the prisoner is so sick or infirm that the production would endanger his life or health. (C. P. A. §1246.) Refusal or neglect without cause to obey the writ is punished by im prisonment until discharged. (C. P. A. §§1248-1250.) These writs may not be disobeyed on the ground of any defect in form. (C. P. A. §1240.)

§541. Hearing. 

The court or judge before whom the prisoner is brought on a writ of habeas corpus must immediately examine into the cause of imprisonment or restraint. He is confined to the question of whether or not the committing justice had jurisdiction to commit the prisoner and may not inquire into the justice thereof. He must make final order to discharge, bail or remand him. (C. P. A. §§1251-1262.) $542. Appeal.

An appeal may be taken from an order refusing to grant a writ or to discharge or remand the prisoner or to dismiss the proceedings. (C. P. A. §1274.) $543. Certiorari Proceedings.

Section 1283 of the C. P. A. states that the writ of certiorari has been abolished, but the sections discussed above (§§1230-1282) specifically provide for a writ of that name.

The contradiction has been resolved by the courts. 

Before the C. P. A. came into existence two different proceedings were both referred to as writs of certiorari-one being a proceeding to review and the other a proceeding to examine and inquire into the cause of detention. The writ to examine and inquire into the cause of detention is specifically preserved under §1230; the writ to review has been supplanted by the order of certiorari. Peo. ex rel. Semenoff v. Nagle, 118 Misc. 476, 194 N. Y. S. 602. 

§544. Certiorari to Review. 

A certiorari proceeding on order is a review of the judicial act or ruling of a body, board, court or officer from which no appeal is allowed. (C. P. A. §1283.) 

Certiorari proceedings are appropriate only to review the judicial action of inferior courts or bodies exercising judicial functions. When the action of a pub-

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

303 lic officer or body is merely legislative, executive or administrative, although it may involve the exercise of discretion, it can not be reviewed by certiorari. Peo. 

ex rel. Trustees of Jamaica v. Bd. of Supervisors, 131 

N. Y. 468, 30 N. E. 488.

With the exception of the writ to inquire into the detention of a prisoner and the writ of certiorari under the tax laws and others outside the Civil Practice Act, the relief formerly obtained by writ is now obtained by order. (C. P. 

A. §1283.) The provisions of the Civil Practice Act are not applicable to a determination made in any criminal matter, except a criminal contempt of court. (C. P. A. §1312.) $545. Right to Certiorari Order. 

The certiorari order is granted to review the determination of a body or officer; and can be granted only: (1) Where the statute confers the right. (2) Where the right existed at common law, by a court of general jurisdiction, and has not been expressly taken away by statute. (C. P. A. §1284.) 

This order can not be granted to review a determination made in a civil action or proceeding, by a court of record or a judge thereof. (C. P. A. §1285.) Except where otherwise specially provided this order cannot be used: (1) To review a determination which is not final. (2) Where the determination can be adequately reviewed by an appeal to a court or to some other body or officer. (3) Where the body or officer making the determination is expressly authorized to hold a rehearing on application, unless the determination was made upon a rehearing, or a rehearing has been denied, or the time during which a rehearing might have been procured has elapsed. (C. P. 

A. §1286.)

$546. Application for the Order.

Application must be made by or on behalf of the person aggrieved, upon a verified petition. Upon the application of a person specially and beneficially interested in the determination to be reviewed, the court, in its discretion, may admit him as a party. (C. P. A. §§1290; 1301.) The order can be granted only at a term of the appellate division of the supreme court or a special term and the granting and notice are discretionary with the court. (C. P. A. §§1287; 1290.) $547. Time.

The time during which the order must be granted and served is four months after the determination to be reviewed becomes final and binding. But where the petitioner or the person whom he represents was at the time the determination became final and binding: (1) within the age of 21 years; (2) insane; or (3) imprisoned on a criminal charge, or on conviction of a criminal offense, for a term less than life; the order may be granted twenty months after the expiration of the usual period of four months. (C. P. A. §§1288, 1289.) 

$548. The Order.

The certiorari order must be directed against the body or officer whose determination is to be reviewed, and served upon each person against whom it is directed. It must also be entered in the office of the clerk of the county where the order is returnable. (C. P. A. §§1292, 1293, 1294.) $549. The Return. 

A certiorari order must be made returnable 20 days after service. The return is made by the persons served by filing in the office designated a copy of the order, a certified transcript of the record of the proceedings and other matter required by the order. (C. P. A. §§1296, 1297.)

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

305 

8550. The Hearing. 

The hearing must be had at a term of the appellate division in the return district. Either party may notice the cause for hearing. Only the following questions involving the merits may be determined: (1) Whether the body or officer had jurisdiction of the subject matter. (2) Whether the authority conferred upon the body or officer in relation to the subject matter has been pursued in the mode required by law in order to authorize it or him to make the determination. (3) Whether, in making the determination, any rule of law affecting the rights of the parties has been violated to the prejudice of the petitioner. (4) Whether there was competent proof of the necessary facts in order to authorize the making of the determination. (5) Or, if there was such proof, whether there was such a preponderance of proof against the existence of those facts, that the determination should be set aside as against the weight of evidence. (C. P. A. §§1300; 1304.) §551. Final Order. 

The court, upon the hearing, may make a final order annulling or confirming, wholly or partly, or modifying, the determination reviewed, as to any or all of the parties. (C. P. A. §1305.) The final order must be entered in the office of the clerk where the order was returnable. (C. P.

A. §1308.) 

8552. Certiorari under the Tax Law. 

Under the Tax Law, another type of certiorari proceeding is allowed. Any person claiming to be aggrieved by a tax assessment may, within 15 days of the filing of the

assessment-roll and first publication of notice thereof, petition the supreme court for a writ of certiorari. Such person must first apply to the assessment officers for a correction of the assessment. If the request is not granted the writ will be allowed on proper proof. The hearing is had at special term and in this case new testimony may be taken. The final order may correct, modify or confirm the original determination. An appeal may be taken to the appellate divi sion. (Tax Law §§290-307.) §553. Mandamus Proceeding. 

A mandamus order is used to compel an inferior court, an officer or domestic corporation to perform a ministerial duty of a public nature in a particular manner. It cannot be used to enforce private rights. Domestic corporations are subject to mandamus because of the visitorial power of the state. (C. P. A. §1313.) 

"The primary object of mandamus is to compel action. It neither creates nor confers powers to act, but only commands the exercise of powers already existing, when it is the duty of the person or body proceeded against to act without its agency. While it may require the performance of a purely ministerial duty in a particular manner, its command is never given to compel the discharge of a duty involving the exercise of judgment or discretion, in any specified way, for that would substitute the judgment or discretion of the court issuing the writ for that of the person or persons against whom the writ was issued." Peo. ex rel. Harris v. Commissioners, 149 N. Y. 26, 30, 43 N. E. 418.

Any citizen may institute mandamus proceedings to compel a public officer to perform duties of a public character. Heeran v. Scully, 135 Misc. 874, 240 N. Y.

S. 2. 

A mandamus order may be obtained to compel the reinstatement of a member wrongfully expelled from an association, and it is not necessary to first exhaust his remedies under the by-laws of the association. Peo.

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

307 

ex rel. Deverell v. Musical Mutual Protective Union, 118 

N. Y. 101, 23 N. E. 129. 

Mandamus may be used to compel city authorities to issue a license to sell pasteurized milk, if such license has been refused unreasonably. Lang's Creamery v. 

City of Niagara Falls, 251 N. Y. 343, 167 N. E. 464; Peo. ex rel. Lodes v. Dept. of Health of N. Y. C., 189 N. Y. 

187, 82 N. E. 187. 

§554. Alternate or Peremptory Order. 

A mandamus order is either alternative or peremptory. 

The alternative order is issued when there are questions of fact, as well as questions of law, to be established. The alternative order commands the defendant to do a specified act or show cause why it should not be done. The peremptory order is granted when only a question of law exists, and commands the defendant to perform a specified act. 

The best practice is to petition for a peremptory order and, in the same petition, for an alternative order in the alternative. If the facts are conceded, the peremptory order will be granted; if they are not conceded, then an alternative order may be granted. (C. P. A. §§1314, 1315; 1319.)

A peremptory mandamus order was granted to compel the city comptroller to pay for easements under land required for subway purposes. Trinity Church v. Berry, 248 N. Y. 564, 162 N. E. 526, aff’ing 223 App. Div. 742, 227 N. Y. S. 564. 

A peremptory mandamus order will be granted to compel a building inspector to approve plans and to issue a building permit. Oppenheimer v. Kraus, 246 N. 

Y. 559, 159 N. E. 651, aff'ing 221 App. Div. 773, 223 N. 

Y. S. 467. 

8555. Application for the Order.

Application must be made, on notice, and upon a verified petition showing a cause of action. Statements are made as in a complaint. Except where the order is directed against a judge of the supreme court, when application is made to

the appellate division of the district, a mandamus order can be granted only at a special term of the supreme court. 

(C. P. A. §§1315-1319.) An alternative order must be en tered in the clerk's office where the return must be made; and served on the defendant, with a copy of the petition. 

(C. P. A. §§1320, 1321.) $556. The Return. 

A return to an alternative order is in the nature of an answer and must be made, on notice, 20 days after service of the order. Instead of a return, the defendant may file written objections in point of law or fact. A return must be made to a peremptory order. Failure to return is punishable as a contempt of court. (C. P. A. §§1322-1329.) 

$557. Motion to Vacate.

A motion to vacate or set aside an alternative order cannot be made for any matter involving the merits. A motion to set it aside for other cause or to set aside or quash a peremptory order, or to set aside the service of either may be made. (C. P. A. §1330.) $558. Procedure.

After issue is joined, the proceedings are the same as in an action. An issue of fact may be tried by a jury, unless a jury is waived. (C. P. A. §§1332, 1333.) The final order in the proceedings is always a peremptory order, which has the force of a final judgment and must be filed with the county clerk. (C. P. A. §§1332; 1320.) An appeal may be taken as in a special proceeding. (C. P. A. §1337.) 

8559. Prohibition Proceeding.

A prohibition order may be granted to restrain a lower 

¹ See p. 216.

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

309 court, or a judge thereof, from exceeding his jurisdiction.

(C. P. A. §1341.) 

"Prohibition does not issue as a matter of right but only in the sound discretion of the court in cases of supreme necessity where the grievance cannot be redressed by ordinary proceedings at law or in equity or by appeal." Peo. ex rel. Hummel v. Trial Term, 184 N. 

Y. 30, 32, 76 N. E. 732.

Prohibition has never been extended to restrain administrative acts. Burke v. Richmond, 131 Misc. 588, 228 N. Y. S. 172.

The Court of Appeals granted an order of prohibition commanding Mr. Justice Scudder to desist from further action as a justice of the supreme court in the investigation of the charges against Maurice Connelly, President of the Borough of Queens. It was held that the function of judges is to determine controversies between litigants. They are not adjuncts or advisors, much less investigating instrumentalities. Their pronouncements are not subject to review by the governor or legislature. The legislature has no power to charge a justice of the supreme court with the duties of a prosecutor in aid of the executive. Nor can such judges act as individuals, since they are prohibited from so doing by Sec. 34 of the Public Officers Law. Richard son v. Scudder, and Connelly v. Scudder, 247 N. Y. 401, 160 N. E. 655. 

§560. Alternative or Final Order. 

A prohibition order is either alternative or final. (C. P. 

A. §1341.) As a rule, a final order cannot be granted until an alternative order has been issued, duly served and the return day has passed. The alternative order must be directed against the court in which, or the judge before whom, the proceedings to be restrained were taken or are about to be taken; and also against the party in whose favor they were or will be taken. It must command the defendants to desist from further proceedings until further direction, and also to show cause why they should not be

absolutely restrained from further proceedings. (C. P. A. §§1351; 1345.) 

§561. Application for the Order. 

Application must be on verified petition and notice. The order must be granted by the supreme court at special term, unless the defendant is a judge of the supreme court, when application is made to the appellate division of the district. 

The order and a copy of the papers must be served on the defendants. (C. P. A. §§1342, 1343, 1344; 1346.) 

§562. The Return. 

A return must be made forthwith or at a day certain and delivered in open court or filed in the office of the county clerk. Where the party makes a return, the court or judge must also make a return. When the court or judge makes a return the party may adopt it as his own. When no return is made and the alternative order has been duly served, the petitioner is entitled to a final order containing an absolute prohibition. (C. P. A. §§1347, 1348; 1350.) 

§563. Proceedings after Return. 

Where the alternative order has been granted, the cause may be disposed of without further notice at the term at which the order is returnable. If it is not then disposed of, it may be brought to a hearing upon notice at a subsequent term. Issues of fact may be tried by a jury. (C. P. A. §1349.) 

§564. Final Order. 

Where a final order is granted in favor of the petitioner it must include an absolute prohibition and must direct that all or any one of the proceedings to which the prohibition relates, be vacated and annulled. Where the final order is against the petitioner, it must authorize the defendants to

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

311 proceed as if the alternative order had not been granted. (C. P. A. §1353.) An appeal from the final order may be taken. (C. P. A. §1354.) 

8565. Quo Warranto.

Upon his own information or upon the complaint of a private person, the attorney general may maintain an action against a person who usurps, intrudes into or unlawfully holds or exercises within the state, a franchise or a public office, civil or military, or an office in a domestic corpora tion. C. P. A. §§1208-1221 provide for such actions. 

In order to determine which of two sets of officers and directors of a corporation were lawfully elected to office, the rights must be determined by an action at law in the nature of quo warranto, and an action in equity to enjoin one set of officers from functioning will not lie. Merchants Loan & Invest. Corp. v. Abramson, 242 N. Y. 587, 152 N. E. 438.

The validity of the appointment of municipal court clerks, deputies and assistants cannot be tried in a taxpayer's action to restrain the payment of their salaries, but must be tried in quo warranto. Spencer v. Leary, 241 N. Y. S. 388. 

8566. Arbitration Proceeding.

If a contract provides for arbitration of differences arising thereunder, such provision is valid and irrevocable and an action cannot be resorted to except on grounds which permit a revocation of a contract at law or in equity. (Arb. 

L. §2.) 

The Arbitration statutes are constitutional with respect to both the state and federal Constitutions. Matter of Berkowitz v. Arbib & Haulberg, Inc., 230 N. Y. 

261, 130 N. E. 288. "The provisions of the Arbitration Law are properly applicable to any contract where the parties have agreed to substitute for the courts an informal tribunal

of their choice in the settlement of a controversy, but they are not applicable where the parties have agreed only to permit third parties to decide a particular matter instead of attempting to reach an agreement themselves." Matter of Fletcher, 237 N. Y. 440, 451, 143 

N. E. 248. 

§567. Compelling Arbitration. 

A party aggrieved by the failure, neglect or refusal of another to submit to such arbitration may petition on notice to the supreme court, or a judge thereof, for an order directing arbitration. If no arbitrators or umpires were named in the contract, or a vacancy has occurred, upon application of either party, such person or persons may be designated by the court or the judge. (Arb. L. §§3, 4.) If the agree ment to arbitrate or the default is disputed, the court must try the issue forthwith. The supreme court, or a judge thereof, has jurisdiction to stay a trial or proceeding brought in violation of such agreement. (Arb. L. §§4, 5.) 

The court has no authority under Arbitration Law §4 to appoint an arbitrator under an agreement between a railroad and the union of its employees during a wage dispute where the rate of wage to be fixed is not the fair market value, but some compromise value, since its authority extends only to justiciable controversies. 

No power is given to the courts to change or modify existing contracts or to make new ones for the parties. 

In re Buffalo & Erie Ry. Co., 250 N. Y. 275, 165 N. E. 291.

When there is a dispute as to whether or not the parties agreed to arbitrate, the court is without authority to appoint arbitrators or to direct the parties to proceed to arbitration. The court should first determine whether the parties had entered into a contract to arbitrate. In re Fang, 227 App. Div. 766, 237 N. Y. 

S. 366.

The city court of the city of New York has no power to stay a trial until the subject matter has been decided by arbitration in compliance with submission under §5 of the Arbitration Law. Kipp v. Hamburg- 

American Line, 134 Misc. 481, 235 N. Y. S. 450.

SPECIAL PROCEEDINGS 

313 

8568. Submission to Arbitrators.

If such provision was not made in the contract, but a controversy which may be the subject of an action exists, two or more persons may submit to arbitration of one or more arbitrators, except: (1) Where one of the parties is an infant or a person incompetent to manage his affairs. (2) Where the controversy arises respecting a claim to an estate in real property in fee or for life. (C. P. A. §1448; 

Mun. Ct. Code §8.) $569. The Submission. 

The submission must be in writing and acknowledged and certified as a deed. It may provide that a judgment of a specified court be entered on the award. If the submission so requires, a hearing on notice must be had. The arbitrators must be sworn. They have the powers of a board authorized to hear testimony. An award by a majority is valid unless the submission requires the concurrence of all. 

(C. P. A. §§1449; 1451, 1452, 1453.) 

Where one of the parties and one of the arbitrators withdraw and refuse to carry out the arbitration proceedings the two remaining arbitrators have no power to proceed with the arbitration and to make and sign an award, without an application under the Arbitration Law to compel specific performance of the contract to arbitrate. Bullard v. Grace Co., 240 N. Y. 388, 148 N. E. 559. "The scheme of the law divides the arbitration proceedings into two parts: (a) the hearing, and (b) the decision and award. All the arbitrators must hear the allegations and proofs of the parties but an award by a majority of them is valid unless the submission otherwise provides. It follows that the withdrawal of one of the arbitrators just before a formal award does not end the authority of the other two to make an award, unless the terms of the arbitration submission takes the case out of the general rule governing majority

awards." Matter of A. E. Fire Ins. Co. v. N. J. Ins. 

Co., 240 N. Y. 398, 399, 148 N. E. 562. 

8570. Motions on the Award.

A motion may be made to confirm, modify, correct, or vacate an award. A motion to correct or modify must be granted if the award shows a mistake, is upon matter not submitted, or is imperfect in form. The award must be vacated if procured by corruption or fraud, or where there was partiality or misconduct on the part of the arbitrators. 

(C. P. A. §§1456, 1457, 1458.) 

Where an arbitrator made an investigation for himself after the hearing and without notice to the parties and based his decision on such investigation as well as on the testimony, his conduct constituted misbehavior for which the award must be vacated. Berizzi Co. v. 

Krausz, 239 N. Y. 315, 146 N. E. 436. 

§571. Judgment. 

Upon the granting of an order confirming, modifying or correcting an award, judgment may be entered in conformity therewith. Such judgment has the same force and effect as a judgment in an action. (C. P. A. §§1461-1464.) §572. Other Special Proceedings. 

Other special proceedings include: (1) the appointment of a guardian of an infant; (2) the appointment of a committee of the person or property, or both, of an incompetent; (3) the sale of real property of an infant or incompetent; (4) adoption; (5) dispossess.2 (Rules 290-294;

C. P. A. §§1356-1384; Rules 285-288; C. P. A. §§1385-1408; 

Rules 295-300; Dom. Rel. L. §§110-118; C. P. A. §§14101447; 1436-a.) (6) Special proceedings relative to incompetent veterans and infant wards of the United States Veterans Bureau are now provided for under Art. 81-A, 

§§1384-a to 1384-s, of the Civil Practice Act. 

2 See p. 279.

CHAPTER XV COSTS §573. Nature of Costs. 

A successful litigant is ordinarily entitled to reimbursement for the expenses necessarily incurred in maintaining his rights before the court. The sums allowed are called costs. The award is incorporated in the order or judgment. 

§574. Source of the Right to Costs. 

The right to costs is created by statute and unless some legislative enactment prescribes costs in a particular case, none may be granted. (Thorndike & Hix Lobster Co. v. 

Hall, 132 Misc. 723, 230 N. Y. S. 554; Matter of the City of Brooklyn, 148 N. Y. 107, 42 N. E. 413; Osborn v. Cardeza, 208 N. Y. 131, 101 N. E. 806.) $575. On a Judgment.

Upon the rendering of a final judgment in his favor, the plaintiff is ordinarily entitled to costs. (C. P. A. §1470.) 

The amounts to be taxed are specified in §1504, and additional amounts in real property action, in §§1512, 1512-a, 1513, 1514 of the Civil Practice Act and §166 of the Municipal Court Code. Disbursements which may be included are specified in §§1518 and 1519 of the Civil Practice Act and 

§170 of the Municipal Court Code. If a defendant is served with a notice subscribed by the plaintiff's attorney setting forth the object of the action, a description of the property affected, if it affects specific real or personal property, and that a personal claim is not made against him, and he unreasonably defends the action, costs may be awarded against him. (C. P. A. §1478.) The municipal court code specifies that a party may recover costs if it is shown that he was represented by an attorney. (Mun. Ct. Code §164.) 315

8576. Limitations.

In an action to recover damages for assault, battery, false imprisonment, libel, slander, criminal conversation, seduction or malicious prosecution, if the plaintiff recovers less than fifty dollars, the amount of his costs may not exceed the damages. (C. P. A. §1471.) In an action to recover a chattel, if the value of the chattel added to the damages is less than fifty dollars, the costs may not exceed the value of the chattel plus the damages. (C. P. A. §1473.) 

§577. When Plaintiff Is Not Entitled to Costs. 

The plaintiff may recover no costs or disbursements: (1) In an action brought in the supreme court in the city of New York, which might have been brought, except for the amount claimed, in the city court, and in which the defendant was served with process within the city of New 

York, unless he recovers two thousand dollars or more. (2) In an action brought in the city court, which might have been brought, except for the amount claimed, in the municipal court, unless he recovers five hundred dollars or more. (3) In an action brought in the supreme court of New 

York or Bronx Counties, for an accounting by or against an executor, administrator, trustee, or guardian or for the construction of a will, for which a proceeding might have been brought in the surrogate's court of such county. (C. P. A. §1474.) 1 If the defendant offered to allow judgment to be entered against him ¹ before trial, the plaintiff cannot recover costs unless the judgment was more favorable than the offer, and must pay costs from the time of the offer. (C. P. 

A. §§173; 177.) In a municipal court action, the plaintiff is not entitled to costs if the judgment did not exceed the 

1 See p. 197.

COSTS 

317 offer, but no provision is made for payment of costs by plaintiff. (Mun. Ct. Code §81.) 

Where the judgment for each plaintiff in a supreme court action for personal injuries was less than $250, the plaintiffs are not entitled to costs. Scioldoni v. Republic Light, Heat & Power Co., 251 N. Y. 574, 168 

N. E. 432.

Where parties are severally liable upon the same written instrument, including a bill of exchange or a promissory note, if plaintiff brings two or more actions against persons who might have been joined as defendants in one action, costs other than disbursements cannot be recovered in more than one action. He may elect the action in which costs are to be assessed. This provision does not apply: (1) if plaintiff joines in each action brought all persons liable and not previously sued, who with reasonable diligence can be found within the state; (2) if the action is brought in the city court; or (3) if the action is brought in the county court within the city or county where the court is located. (C. P. A. §1479.) 

8578. The Defendant's Right to Costs. 

Ordinarily, if the plaintiff is not entitled thereto, the defendant is entitled to costs as of course. (C. P. A. §1475.) 

But if the plaintiff is not entitled to costs because he might have brought the action in a lower court, the defendant is not entitled to costs. (C. P. A. §1475.) When the plaintiff is entitled to costs against one or more defendants, but not against all, none of the defendants is entitled to costs as of course. In this case, the court, in its discretion, may award costs to any defendant against whom the plaintiff is not entitled to costs, if he did not unite in an answer and was not united in interest with a defendant against whom the plaintiff is entitled to costs. (C. P. A. §1476.) See Mun.

Ct. Code §164. 

1

Defendant The Mirror brought the Rockford Dairy, Inc., into an action as defendant and thereby subjected it to the peril of a judgment against it. Judgment was entered in favor of the Rockford Dairy, Inc., and it was entitled to costs against The Mirror because of plaintiff's demand in the summons. Lane v. The Mirror, 134 Misc. 767, 236 N. Y. S. 273. See also Kohlberg v. Hollaran, 133 Misc. 304, 231 N. Y. S. 406. $579. Costs to Either Party.

Unless the right to costs is specially prescribed, the court, in its discretion, may award costs on final judgment to any party in a sum not exceeding the statutory limitation. (C. P. A. §1477.) In an action in which the plaintiff would ordinarily be entitled to costs as of course, if the plaintiff recovers on one or more of the issues and the defendant on the other or others, each is entitled to costs against the adverse party; unless it is certified that the substantial cause of action was the same upon each issue, when the plaintiff only is entitled to costs. (C. P. A. §1483.) 

See Mun. Ct. Code §164. 

Two causes of action were separately stated and numbered in the complaint. The defendant recovered on the first and the plaintiff on the second. Each was entitled to costs on his recovery. Browning v. N. Y., L. E. & W. R. R. Co., 64 Hun 513, 19 N. Y. S. 453. 

When both the complaint and the counterclaim for a larger amount are dismissed, the plaintiff is entitled to costs as the prevailing party under §164 of the Municipal Court Code. Kohlberg v. Halloran, 133 Misc. 304, 231 N. Y. S. 406. $580. Liability of a Guardian ad Litem.

In the supreme, county and city courts a guardian ad litem for an infant is not liable for costs unless specially charged therewith by order of the court. (C. P. A. §205.)

The guardian ad litem of an infant plaintiff in the municipal court must consent to be liable for costs if he fails in the

COSTS 

319 action. If a guardian ad litem for an infant defendant in the municipal court is appointed on application of the plaintiff or on the court's own motion, he is not liable for costs. 

(Mun. Ct. Code §26.) 

8581. On Motion.

Costs may be awarded on a motion, but they are usually in the discretion of the court. (C. P. A. §§1486, 1487, 1488; 

1505; Rule 115; Mun. Ct. Code §167.) $582. On Appeal.

In an action wherein plaintiff is entitled to costs as of course, the successful party on appeal is entitled to costs; except that where a new trial is directed, costs may be awarded to either party, absolutely or to abide the event, in the discretion of the court. In other cases the award is in the discretion of the court. (C. P. A. §§1490, 1491; 1508, 1509, 1510.) On appeal from the municipal court, costs are discretionary. (Mun. Ct. Code §172.) 

§583. In a Special Proceeding. 

Unless prescribed, costs in a special proceeding may be awarded as in an action in the discretion of the court. (C. 

P. A. §1492.) In a supplementary proceeding, the judge may make an order granting costs to the judgment creditor. 

If no property was discovered in the supplementary proceeding, the judge may grant costs to the judgment debtor. 

(C. P. A. §§803, 804.) §584. Security. 

The defendant may require that security for costs be given where the plaintiff when the action was commenced was: (1) a nonresident; or (2) a foreign corporation; or (3) a prisoner or his official assignee; the official assignee or official trustee of a debtor; or an official assignee in bank-

ruptcy; where the action is brought upon a cause of action arising before the assignment, the appointment of the trustee or the adjudication in bankruptcy; or (4) in a city court action, if the plaintiff does not live or have a place of business in the city. Also where, after the commencement of the action, the plaintiff either: (5) becomes a nonresident; (6) is adjudicated a bankrupt or discharged from his debts, or exonerated from imprisonment; or (7) is sentenced to the state prison for a term less than life; (8) in a city court action, ceases to be a resident or have a place of business within the city. The defendant cannot require security for costs unless he is entitled to require it of all the plaintiffs. 

(C. P. A. §1522; City Ct. Act §53.) (9) If the summons, in an action against a nonresident, was served as prescribed in §52 of the Vehicle and Traffic Law, the plaintiff may be required to give security for costs. (Vehicle & Traffic L. §53.) It is within the discretion of the court to require the plaintiff to give security for costs in an action by or against: an executor or administrator, the trustee of an express trust, a person expressly authorized by statute to sue or to be sued, an official assignee, the assignee of a receiver, or the committee of a person judicially declared to be incom pètent. (C. P. A. §1523.) 

$585. Liability of an Attorney. 2 Where a defendant is entitled to security for costs under (1), (4) and (9) supra, the plaintiff's attorney is liable for the defendant's costs to an amount not exceeding $100 until security is given. The attorney may relieve himself of the liability by filing an undertaking. (C. P. A. §1530; 

Vehicle & Traffic L. §53.) 

8586. Taxation.

Costs are taxed by the clerk on application by the party entitled thereto, except that the court may direct that inter- 1 

2 See p. 87.

COSTS 

321 locutory costs or costs in a special proceeding be taxed by a judge. (C. P. A. §1532; Mun. Ct. Code §169.) In the supreme and county courts, 5 days' notice of such taxation must be given the attorney for the adverse party, unless both attorneys have their offices in the city or town where the costs are to be taxed, when a notice of 2 days is sufficient. A copy of the itemized bill of costs, with disbursements stated in detail, must be served with the notice. (C. P. A. §1534.) In the city court, taxation is made on 

2 days' notice, except where the attorneys served have their office in the city of New York, when one day's notice is sufficient. (City Ct. Act §29.) Taxation in the municipal court is made on 2 days' notice. (Mun. Ct. Code §169.)
